### THE
# WAY to CHRIST,
## Described
### In the following TREATISES:

| | |
|---|---|
| *Of True Repentance.* | *Of Regeneration.* |
| *Of True Resignation.* | *Of the Super-Sensual Life.* <note>original: "Super-Sensual Life" refers to a life existing above or beyond the reach of the physical senses.</note> |

### Written in the Year 1622,
## BY JACOB BOEHME,
### of *Goerlitz* in Northern Germany.

---

> "Be Earnest—Be Earnest."

---

## CANTERBURY:
->Republished, with an *Illustrated Memoir*,<-
->by G. MORETON, 42, *Burgate Street*, 1894.<-

---

->(Price Seven Shillings.)<-

--- Page 8 ---
# PREFATORY MEMOIR.

---

The materials available for describing the life of Jacob Boehme <note>original: "Jacob Behmen"</note> are quite limited. They consist for the most part of a few surprising incidents, some of which would be considered legendary or unbelievable if they were not so well-documented and narrated by Boehme himself. These incidents, more or less rephrased, have served as the basis for biographies by successive editors of his works.

Jacob Boehme, though a shoemaker by trade, was a very remarkable man and became the greatest theosopher <note>A philosopher who seeks direct knowledge of God and the world through spiritual ecstasy or divine illumination.</note> who has ever lived. His writings cannot be understood without considerable effort and a spiritual sympathy with his ideas; consequently, they have seemed like foolishness to many, and only a few have discovered what has been called the "Pearl" hidden within them.

Regarding Jacob Boehme’s birth and parentage, it is known that he was born in the year 1575 at Alt-Seidenberg (Old Seidenberg), a small market town in Northern Germany. It is now situated on the border of the Prussian frontier and within two miles of the principal town of Görlitz, which at that time was a place of much importance and remains a significant business center today. He was named after his father, and his mother’s name was Ursula. He was not their only child, although neither the names of the other children nor any mention of them appears to be recorded, aside from a reference to a "dead brother." As a boy, he was employed with other lads to tend cattle and perform similar chores that could be found for him near home. Even at that age, he appears to have been of a pensive and dreamy disposition, much inclined to reverie. He did not have a robust constitution, a trait that continued even into his manhood.

One of the earliest recorded incidents of Jacob’s life occurred while he was herding cattle. It is told that one day at noon, having left his companions to look after the herd, he went to a nearby hill called the Land’s Crown. There, a rock formation bore some resemblance to a cave. Finding an opening through the brushwood, he entered and saw a large wooden vessel filled with money. The sight so alarmed him that he ran back to his companions and told them what happened; but although they returned with him immediately, both the entrance and the fairy-like money had disappeared. Some years later, as Jacob Boehme him—

<summary>This preface introduces the life of the German mystic Jacob Boehme, noting his humble origins as a shoemaker and a mysterious childhood encounter with a hidden treasure.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, biography, theosophy, Görlitz, Seidenberg, childhood, mysticism, Land's Crown</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the biographical account of Jacob Boehme's early life and the mystical events that shaped his future.</meta>

# Prefatory Memoir.

As he himself narrates, a "foreign artist" found and appropriated the hidden treasure; and, in consequence, came to an untimely end.

The place of this strange occurrence is still known, and is one of the excursions from *Goerlitz*. It is called the "Landskrone," and is in reality a basaltic hill, on the summit of which an ancient robbers' stronghold formerly stood—which may to some extent account for the hidden treasure—and is now occupied by a castle-like building used as an inn and a "Belvedere" <note>A building designed to provide a beautiful view.</note>, from which an expansive view of the surrounding country is obtained.

As *Jacob* grew up and completed his elementary schooling, his father, not finding him sufficiently strong for agricultural work, apprenticed him to a shoemaker at *Goerlitz*. After he had worked there for some time, another singular incident occurred to him. We are told that while his master had temporarily left him in charge of the shop, a man, who was a stranger to *Jacob*, entered and desired to purchase a pair of shoes. *Jacob*, not venturing to sell anything in his master’s absence, declined to serve him; but the stranger becoming persistent about it, *Jacob* offered them to him at such a high price as would certainly protect him from his master’s blame and would probably discourage the stranger from purchasing. The stranger, however, paid the price demanded and took the shoes. Having left the shop, he called to the lad, "*Jacob*, come outside," or, as some of his biographers more dramatically render it, "*Jacob, Jacob*, come forth!" 

The lad did so as soon as he had recovered from his astonishment, and the stranger addressed him: "*Jacob*, you are as yet but small, but the time will come when you shall be great and become another man, and the world shall marvel at you. Therefore, be pious, fear God, and reverence His Word; especially read the Bible diligently where you have comfort and instruction, for you must endure much misery and poverty, and suffer persecution. But be courageous and persevere, for God loves and is gracious to you." The stranger then pressed his hand, gave him a kind look, and departed—with the shoes.

It is questionable whether these incidents were visionary experiences rather than actual occurrences to JACOB BOEHME; but the result of them was that his thoughts assumed a deeply religious form—far deeper than that of the mere "Lutheran Faith" in which he had been brought up. This operated upon him so strongly in the years to come that his spirit was able to penetrate into the innermost mysteries of nature: to foresee and foretell what is now known as electricity, with its immense possibilities; and also to divine the three great principles or first laws of nature—attraction, equal resistance producing orbicular <note>original: "orbicular" — meaning circular or spherical motion.</note>...

<summary>This page describes Jacob Boehme's apprenticeship to a shoemaker and a prophetic encounter with a mysterious stranger who predicted his future greatness and suffering. These early mystical experiences are credited with sparking Boehme's deep spiritual insights into the laws of nature and science.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Goerlitz, Landskrone, shoemaker apprenticeship, prophetic stranger, mysticism, Lutheranism, laws of nature, electricity</keywords>

# Prefatory Memoir.

movement, resulting in gravitation. These principles were studied by Sir Isaac Newton, who was a diligent though unacknowledged student of *Boehme’s* works. By reducing these ideas to a mathematical form, Newton established that grand theory of the universe—the Solar System—which Thomas Carlyle described as the most perfect approach to a theory that has ever been proposed.

Meanwhile, however, *Jacob* continued his occupation as a shoemaker, although we are told that at times he worked almost unconsciously for days at a time when his thoughts were in devout contemplation. At other times, he did not hesitate to rebuke any coarse conversation in which the other workmen might indulge. This so irritated his master that he discharged him from his service, and *Jacob* was obliged to set out as a traveling journeyman <note>A "journeyman" was a trained worker who practiced his trade by traveling and working for different masters rather than owning his own shop.</note>. In the year 1594, he returned to *Goerlitz*, where he became a master shoemaker and married *Catherine*, daughter of John Hunshman, a butcher of *Goerlitz*.

About six years after his marriage, *Jacob's* first "Illumination" <note>A spiritual awakening or mystical vision.</note> occurred. This was the result, as he says, of strenuously clinging by faith to the promise of the Holy Spirit given to those who seek it. It happened in the following manner:

We are told that while sitting in his room one day, he noticed a burnished pewter dish which reflected the sunlight with such brilliance that he was overcome with an inward ecstasy. It seemed to him as if he could now look into the principles and deepest foundations of things. He initially believed it was only his imagination, and in order to banish it from his mind, he went out into the fields near the town. But there he noticed that he gazed into the very heart of things, even the grass and herbs. He felt that actual Nature harmonized with what he had seen in his vision, and that he could distinguish the essences, uses, and properties of plants in a manner that was, to him, a most wonderful insight or revelation. He is said to have kept his knowledge of this occurrence mostly to himself; returning home, he continued to take care of his family and worked on in silence until the year 1610. At that time, a further Illumination occurred to him, of which he wrote a memorial.

Regarding his insight into these mysteries, he says:
> "I never desired to know anything of the Divine Mystery, much less understood the way to seek and find it. I knew nothing of it, being in the condition of poor laymen in their simplicity. I sought only after the Heart of Jesus Christ, that I might hide myself there from the wrathful anger of God and the violent..."

# Prefatory Memoir.

Assaults of the Devil. And I besought the Lord earnestly for His Holy Spirit and His Grace, that He would please to bless and guide me *in* Him, and take away from me that which turned me from Him; and I resigned myself wholly to Him, that I might not live to my own will, but *His;* and that He only might lead and direct me—to the end that I might be His child in His Son Jesus.

> “In this my earnest and Christian seeking and desire (in which I suffered many a sharp repulse, but at last resolved rather to put myself at risk than give up and leave off) the gate was opened to me, and in one quarter of an hour I saw and knew more than if I had been many years together at a university, at which I marveled exceedingly, and thereupon turned my praise to God for it.
> 
> “For I saw and knew the Being of all beings, the Byss <note>The "Byss" (from the Greek 'byssos') refers to the "ground" or foundation of being, contrasted with the "Abyss" or "Unground."</note> and the Abyss, and the eternal generation of the Holy Trinity, the descent and origin of the world, and of the creatures through the Divine Wisdom. I knew and saw in myself all three worlds, namely, the Divine, Angelic, and Paradisiacal; and the Dark World, the origin of nature toward fire; and then, thirdly, the external and visible world, being a procreation or external birth from both the internal and spiritual worlds. And I saw and knew the whole working essence, in both the evil and the good, and the origin and existence of each of them, and likewise how the fruitful, bearing womb of eternity brought forth.
> 
> “So that I did not only wonder greatly at it, but also rejoiced exceedingly, and soon it came powerfully into my mind to set the same down in writing, for a memorial for myself, though I could scarcely comprehend the same in my external self and express it with the pen.”

The memorial which JACOB BOEHME wrote of his first illumination is entitled “Aurora, or the Morning Redness;” but before the manuscript was completed, it was seen by CARL VON ENDERN, a nobleman of philosophical tastes, who, being very much interested in it, prevailed upon *Boehme* to lend it to him. Having obtained the loan of it, he had several copies of it made, which thus got into general circulation. A copy also came into the possession of one who became a bitter persecutor of JACOB BOEHME, and who in that relation will ever be associated with him—GREGORY RICHTER, the Primate or chief clergyman of *Goerlitz*, whose lifelong resentment against *Boehme* appears to have already originated in an incident as ludicrous as it was trivial, which is related as follows:

It appears that a baker in *Goerlitz*, desiring to make some cakes for sale at Eastertide, and not having any money with

# Prefatory Memoir.

with which to buy the necessary meal, borrowed a Dollar from the Primate for that purpose. The cakes were made; and having sent the Minister a large one as a thank offering, the baker sold the remainder and was able, within a fortnight, to repay the loan. But the Minister, having expected usury even for so small a sum and so short a period, and being a very passionate man, was greatly incensed against the poor baker, and, cursing him, threatened his soul with condemnation. The poor baker returned home much dejected and in superstitious despair of his salvation, and, his wife being related to **JACOB BOEHME**, told *Jacob* of the incident and begged his intercession with the Primate, to whom *Jacob* at once went and interceded, offering compensation for any injury sustained.

The Primate, on hearing of *Jacob's* mission, in great wrath ordered him to leave the house, which order *Jacob* at once proceeded to obey, praying God to protect his Worship; but he had scarcely reached the door of the room when his Worship, who appeared to have been angered at *Jacob's* goodness and humility, threw his shoe at him as he departed. *Jacob* at once picked it up, and with much reverence and humility laid it at the Primate’s feet, and with another prayer for his Worship departed, leaving him in a state of great anger.

The following *Sunday* the Primate reviled *Jacob* by name from the pulpit, and informed the assembled and worshiping Council—much to their alarm—that unless **JACOB BOEHME** were expelled from the city, God in His wrath would destroy it as He did those who withstood *Moses* and *Aaron*. To all of this, *Jacob*, who was a regular worshiper at the church, listened with patience; and after the service, with much humility, asked the Primate in what manner he had offended; but he only abused him and ordered him out of his sight.

The next morning *Jacob* was summoned to appear before the Council, who were in too much fear of the Primate to listen to *Jacob's* defense; and they sentenced him to instant and perpetual banishment, without one moment’s farewell to his wife and family. His reply was: “Yes, dear sirs, it shall be done, since it cannot be otherwise; I am content,” and as one of his biographers says, “he went out of the town, spending the remainder of the day in a melancholy walk about the town fields, and the night in what shelter we do not know.”

But the next morning the Councilors, having somewhat recovered from their fear of the Primate and considering that they had acted with undue severity against a man whose gentle bearing had evidently won their hearts, revoked the sentence; and sent for **JACOB BOEHME**, who was brought back into the

Despite the Primate's anger, the Council soon regrets their decision and revokes the sentence due to Boehme's humble character.</summary>

# Prefatory Memoir.

Town with honor. They, however, requested him to deliver up his manuscripts, which he did; and they forbade him to write any more, but told him, as a shoemaker, to stick to his last.

This occurrence, which was naturally much discussed, brought JACOB BOEHME into general notice and secured for him the friendship and—as his shoemaking business gradually declined—the financial assistance of many clever and influential men of that time, including doctors and chemists who were attracted by the "Aurora." This was particularly true of DR. WALTHER, Director of the Chemical Laboratory at *Dresden*, who had traveled in the East and who was so attracted by *Jacob's* writings that he came and stayed at his house for three months so that he might enjoy his uninterrupted conversation. It was this DR. WALTHER who proposed the Forty Questions concerning the soul which—along with JACOB BOEHME'S replies, given to the doctor's great satisfaction—were published with *Boehme's* other works. Another important friendship was with DR. KOBER of *Goerlitz*, who later attended JACOB BOEHME'S deathbed.

It is said that from these learned friends JACOB BOEHME acquired the technical Latin terms which he uses in his works; and we are told that when he first heard from them the Greek word "Idea," he became as if electrified and said, "I see a pure and heavenly maiden." This is the maiden whom he designates in his works as Sophia or Wisdom, and "who reflects the glory of God, of the world, and of man."

For several years afterward, *Jacob*, in obedience to the council's injunction, abstained from expressing his thoughts in writing, which was a great hardship to him. He says, 

> "I had resolved to do nothing in the future but to be quiet before God in obedience and to let the Devil with all his host sweep over me. But it was with me as when a seed is hidden in the earth. It grows up in storm and rough weather against all reason. For in wintertime everything is dead, and reason says, *it is all over with it*. But the precious seed within me sprouted and grew green, oblivious to all storms, and amid disgrace and ridicule, it has blossomed forth into a lily."

At the end of seven years, owing to the urgent requests of his friends to share his Light, he resumed his writing, and in the few years remaining before his death, he produced a wonderful series of works.

His book *The Way to Christ* was published by his

# Prefatory Memoir.

friends, and is the only one of all his works that was published during the author’s lifetime. Its publication appears to have further incensed the Primate *Richter*, who attacked JACOB BOEHME from the pulpit and through the press with the greatest abuse,* some of it being too offensive to repeat. To these attacks, JACOB BOEHME published his Defense, written—as one of his biographers remarks—“with an earnestness that inspires holy awe, and, at the same time, with a most hearty gentleness and love”—good feelings which, it is almost needless to say, were entirely lost upon the Primate.

The Primate had again taken advantage of the superstitious fears of the councillors, and JACOB BOEHME was brought before them. They did not dare to accept *Jacob’s* defense and extricated themselves from the difficulty by suggesting to him that, since he had made himself liable to be treated as a heretic by the Emperor, he should go into voluntary exile for the peace of everyone concerned. He did so within two months; but before many months had passed, he returned to *Görlitz* for the last time, unmolested, to die.

On leaving *Görlitz*, he was invited to the Electoral Court at *Dresden*, where he had become well known through his writings. It is stated that the Elector himself much admired him and assembled all the chief theologians and two professors of mathematics for a conference with JACOB BOEHME, so that they might express an opinion about him. After hearing him, DR. GERHARD, who is regarded as a great dogmatist of that time, said: “I would not take the whole world and help to condemn this man;” and one of the other doctors said: “My good brother, neither would I. Who knows what stands behind this man? How can we judge what we have not understood? May God convert the man if he is in error. He is a man of marvelously high mental gifts, who at present can be neither condemned nor approved;” and these eminent men who examined him later became his warmest admirers.

In *England*, the opinion of those who read JACOB BOEHME’S works was similar to that of the theologians. *Charles the First* was a great admirer of them; and it is related that, after he had read *Boehme’s Answers to the Forty Questions concerning the Soul*, he said: “God be praised that there are still men in existence who are able to give a living testimony of God and His Word from their own experience;” and it is to JOHN SPARROW,

His writings also gained significant royal and scholarly admiration in England, including from King Charles I.</summary>

# Prefatory Memoir.

...to a Barrister of the Inner Temple that we owe an excellent translation of *Boehme's* works into English, which he produced between the years 1646 and 1662.

It is stated that HENRY MOORE, a professor at *Cambridge*, was instructed to examine JACOB BOEHME'S works and to report against them. He examined them, but, strange to say, he pronounced in their favor; although he admitted there were some things in them which he did not *then* understand, he added that those who treated *Boehme* with contempt were, in his opinion, ignorant and mentally blind. The professor was convinced that JACOB BOEHME had been specially awakened for the purpose of correcting and guiding those who believed merely in an external Christ, without regard to having the Spirit of Christ within themselves.

In the month of *August* 1624, the Primate GREGORY RICHTER died,* having suffered the humiliation of seeing one of his sons become an ardent disciple of JACOB BOEHME, whose works he had copied and circulated; and a few months later, the man whom the Primate had so ruthlessly reviled and persecuted also died.

Regarding the manner of JACOB BOEHME'S death, it is stated that, while staying with a nobleman in *Silesia*, he fell ill with a fever caused by gastric disease. At his request, he was carried back to *Goerlitz*, where he was attended by his old friend, DR. TOBIAS KOBER. Upon his arrival, he told his friend that he was dying and that he was quite willing to die according to the Will of God, adding: "In three days’ time you will see how God will bring me to my end." This appears to have been on *Thursday*, the 14th of *November*, 1624.†

On the following *Sunday* morning, shortly after midnight, he called his son *Tobias* to him and asked him whether he could hear sounds of sweet music. *Tobias* said he could not; and his father asked him to open the door of the room so that he could hear it better. He then asked the hour, and when told, he said: "My time is not yet; three hours from now is my time"—as it proved to be. A little later he was overheard to say: "Oh, You...

<note>† There appears to be some confusion among his biographers regarding the date of JACOB BOEHME'S death. # Prefatory Memoir.

"strong God of Sabaoth, deliver me according to Your Will," and immediately afterward, "You crucified Lord Jesus Christ, have mercy upon me, and take me to Yourself in Your kingdom."

He afterward gave his wife directions regarding his manuscripts and other matters, and told her that she would not long survive him: a prediction which was fulfilled, as she died of the plague in the following year.

It is very beautifully told that toward the end his spirit seemed to be kept from departing solely by the loving looks of his eldest son, who constantly attended him; but, at six o'clock on *Sunday* morning, the *17th of November*, 1624, he suddenly bid them farewell with a smile and said, "Now I enter Paradise"—the supreme moment arrived—and his spirit was gone.

---

A few months before JACOB BOEHME'S death, he wrote as follows in one of his letters: "That which my native country casts away, other nations will take up." His writings are now universally known, and there is scarcely a civilized country in whose language they may not be found.

In personal appearance JACOB BOEHME is said to have been "somewhat ordinary," of small stature, with a low forehead, prominent temples, and an aquiline nose, a thin beard, gray eyes "sparkling with heavenly blue," and a feeble but genial voice. In his bearing he was modest and unassuming, patient under suffering, and full of gentle humility. His portrait in the Council Hall of *Görlitz*, from which the frontispiece to this volume* has been prepared, scarcely supports this description and may be a modern idealization; it is, however, the officially accepted portrait of him there. There is an engraving by *Wolfe* which appears to be a more realistic portrait of *Boehme*, but nothing can be confirmed regarding its authenticity. It bears German lines by *Angelus*, of which the following is a translation:

> ->*In water lives the fish, the plant in the earth,*<-
> ->*The bird in the air, in the firmament the sun.*<-
> ->*The salamander must subsist in fire,*<-
> ->*And the heart of God is Jacob Boehme's element.*<-

It is grievous to relate that after JACOB BOEHME'S death, the new primate refused his body a decent burial, and the poor

<meta>This page continues the account of Jacob Boehme’s death, detailing the controversies surrounding his funeral and his eventual legacy in the city of Görlitz.</meta>

# Prefatory Memoir.

The Council members of *Görlitz* did not know what to do, faced as they were by the clergy and by the public, whose prejudices had been excited against *Boehme*. They secured the intervention of the Catholic Count Hannibal von Drohna, who ordered the body to be buried in a solemn manner in the presence of two members of the City Council, which was done. It is said that the Primate's last typical act regarding *Boehme* was to take medicine to avoid being forced to preach the funeral sermon; and his deputy began his address by expressing his disgust at the duty he had been called upon to perform.

It is related that some of Jacob Boehme's friends in *Silesia* sent a cross, decorated with occult symbols, for his grave. On the top was a flaming symbol "with a Hebrew inscription signifying IHSVH" and twelve golden rays. Below were the initials of his favorite motto and a picture of a child asleep resting upon a skull, signifying regeneration through the mystic death, with an inscription: "*Here rests Jacob Boehme, born of God; died in Christ, sealed with the Holy Spirit.*" On the right was a black eagle upon a mountain, with the word *Vidi* written beneath it; on the left was a lion, with the word *Vici*; below was a lamb, with the word *Veni*. One of his biographers explains the meaning of these words and symbols this way: "*Veni*, I came into the world; *Vidi*, I saw Satan descend; *Vici*, I conquered the infernal," etc. But others might interpret their meaning as: *Veni*, I came—with the gentleness of a lamb; *Vidi*, I saw—with the swift, piercing vision of an eagle; and *Vici*, I conquered in upholding my convictions—with the courage of a lion.

It is stated that the townspeople soon removed and destroyed this cross; however, their descendants have since done great honor to Jacob Boehme's memory. In the year 1875, on the 300th anniversary of his birth, a great festival was held at *Görlitz*, at which thousands were present. His portrait has been accepted and hung with honor in the Council Hall; his house is marked by a plaque attached to it, recording the fact that he lived and died there; and over his grave there has been placed a huge monumental block which is, like his works, substantial enough to endure for ages to come.

IT has frequently been asserted by very capable commentators on Jacob Boehme that his works are not *essential* to anyone—with the exception of his "*Way to Christ*," which is the least metaphysical of any of them—any more than a knowledge of physics is essential to a farmer; who, without any such knowledge, can cultivate the ground and reap its fruits. In like manner, the Christian can cultivate an

It also introduces a comparison between the necessity of his metaphysical writings and a farmer's need for physics.</summary>

<page-num>xiii</page-num>
<header>Prefatory Memoir.</header>

Holy Spirit and reap *its* fruits without any knowledge of the Mysteries of Nature such as *Boehme* has penetrated and revealed to us.
Jacob Boehme’s works are, as the Rev. W. Law long ago asserted, “just as they should be for those who are fit for them,” and this is the true standpoint from which to regard them. *No one has ever studied them in vain*; and Freher, who is regarded as one of Jacob Boehme’s most able commentators, read all of *Boehme's* books in the original more than ten times over, though not without the greatest disgust imaginable in the beginning.
During Jacob Boehme’s lifetime, he was frequently questioned by his friends and followers in their anxiety respecting the state of the soul immediately after death; and upon this subject, he has written some very impressive passages, such as in a case where the soul, not having passed through regeneration in this life, had at the hour of its departure clung by faith to the promise of forgiveness and salvation. He writes: 
> “When the hour of death approaches and the conscience awakens, such a soul trembles in great fear of hell and would like to be saved; but there is only very little of the saving power of faith within her, while before her is nothing but (a past of) injustice and falsehood, earthly lust, and the tears and sighs of the downtrodden. Her desire turns to a certain extent toward God, but the sins she has committed are in her way, and there arises in her a great doubt and unrest. Many a soul then clings to the saving power, as if by a thin thread. When, then, death actually occurs and separates the soul from the body, the poor soul will then cling to that thread and refuse to let go her hold of it, but all her essences are still deeply immersed in the wrath of God. She is tormented by her gross sins, and the thread of faith (the umbilical cord) of the new-born being is very feeble. Therefore, when the Bridegroom says ‘Come!’ the poor soul answers ‘I cannot, my lamp is not yet ready’; but she clings to the thread and puts her imagination into the heart of God, and thus she becomes ultimately redeemed by the suffering of the Christ in her from the pool of putrefaction.*
> “It is not possible to describe what kind of a purgatory† such a soul will have to pass through before, by means of her little spark of love, she can enter into eternal life. The world would not believe such a description; the world is too clever and likewise too blind to understand it. People cling forever

---

<note>\* “Three Principles.”</note>
<note>† Jacob Boehme was of the “Lutheran” form of belief.</note>

<meta>This page concludes the prefatory memoir, contrasting the lived experience and practical focus of Jacob Boehme's writings with the more sheltered, poetic works of Thomas à Kempis, while urging readers to approach the text with an open and loving mind.</meta>

<page-num>xiv</page-num>
<header>Prefatory Memoir.</header>

to the letter. I wish to God that no one should have to pass through that experience. I would then gladly keep still and say nothing about it.”<note>Answers to Forty Questions.</note>

There is no writer with whom Jacob Boehme can be compared. His “Way to Christ” might suggest to some minds the “Imitation of Christ” written by that good man Thomas à Kempis; but their similarity consists only in the identity of their subject. For the span of seventy-one years, Thomas à Kempis led a calm monastic life, comfortably sheltered from the world and most of its everyday trials. His work, though truly beautiful in its composition, is perhaps more poetical than practical in nature, and is indeed written in meter in the original. Jacob Boehme, on the contrary, had to adhere to his convictions at the loss of his employment and means of sustenance. He was forced into silence regarding mysteries into which he had the deepest insight, and was banished from his home, disgraced and ridiculed. It is therefore not surprising that his work should be of a strenuously practical nature; the very title of his book, the “Way,” rather than the “Imitation,” suggests the activity rather than passivity of its author’s own experiences.

In conclusion: those whom this republication may lead to the study of Jacob Boehme’s works should remember the simple but true maxim—that *where there is no love, there can be no true knowledge*. And if any are inclined—like the unenviably memorable Gregory Richter, Primate of *Goerlitz*—to reject and condemn them, let it not be in *his* manner. Instead, let them bear in mind the cautious tolerance displayed by the old German theologian Dr. Meissner, who, when asked for his opinion of them, replied: “How can we judge what we have not understood?”

->G. B. M.<-

->17th of January, 1894.<-

### THE
# AUTHOR’S PREFACE,
### TO THE
## READER.

DEAR Reader, if you will use this book correctly, and are *in good earnest*, you shall certainly find the benefit of it.—But I desire that you may be warned, if you are not *in earnest*, not to meddle with the *dear names* of <term>GOD</term>, in and by which the most *High Holiness* is invoked, moved, and powerfully desired, lest they kindle the *anger* of <term>GOD</term> in your soul. For we must not abuse the *Holy Names* of <term>GOD</term>.—This little book is only for those who would gladly *repent*, and have a *desire to begin*. Such people will find what kind of words are within it, and from where they are *born*. Be commended herewith to the *eternal goodness and mercy* of <term>GOD</term>.

# OF TRUE REPENTANCE.

---

*How a man must stir himself up in mind and will; and what his consideration and earnest purpose must be, when he intends to perform powerful and effective repentance: and with what mindset he must appear before God, when he would ask for, so as to obtain, the remission of sins.*

When a man intends to enter into *repentance*, and with his prayers *turn to* GOD; he should, before he begins to pray, seriously consider the state of his own *soul*. *How* it is wholly and altogether *turned away from* GOD, become *faithless* to Him, and only bent upon *this temporal, frail, and earthly life*; bearing no sincere love toward GOD and its neighbor, but wholly lusting and walking contrary to the commandments of GOD, and seeking *itself* only, in the temporal and transitory *lusts of the flesh*.

In the next place, he should consider that all this is an *utter enmity against* GOD, which *Satan* has raised and worked in him, by *his deceit in our first parents*; for the sake of which abomination we must suffer death, and undergo corruption with our bodies.

*Thirdly*, he should consider the *three horrible chains* with which our *souls* are fast bound during the time of this earthly life.—The *first* is the severe *anger of* GOD, the *abyss and dark world*, which is the *center, root, or constituent principle of the soul's life*. The *second* is the *desire of the devil* against the soul, by which he continually sifts and tempts it, and without intermission strives to throw it from the truth of GOD into his own evil *nature and element, namely, into pride, covetousness, envy, and anger*; and with his *desire*, blows *up* and kindles those evil *properties* in the soul, whereby its *will* turns away from GOD, and enters into *self*. The *third* and most *harmful chain* of all, with which the poor soul is tied, is the *corrupt and altogether vain, earthly, and mortal flesh and blood*, full of evil desires and inclinations.

Here he must consider that he lies as a close *prisoner* with soul and body in the *mire of sins*, in the *anger of* GOD, in the *jaws of hell*; that the anger of GOD burns in him in soul and body, and that he is that very loathsome *keeper of swine*, who has spent and consumed his father's *inheritance*, namely,

...the precious *Love* and *Mercy* of GOD, with the fatted *swine* of the *Devil* in earthly pleasures, and has not kept the dear *Covenant* and *Atonement* of the innocent *Death* and *Passion* of *Jesus Christ*; which *Covenant* GOD out of pure *Grace* has given or placed into our humanity, and reconciled us in *Him*. He must also consider that he has totally forgotten the *Covenant* of holy *Baptism*, in which he had promised to be faithful and true to his *Savior*, and so completely defiled and obscured His *Righteousness* with *Sin* (which *Righteousness* GOD had freely bestowed upon him in *Christ*), that he now stands before the face of GOD with the fair garment of *Christ’s innocence* which he has defiled, as a *dirty, ragged, and patched keeper* of *swine*, who has continually eaten the *husks of vanity* with the *Devil’s swine*, and is not worthy to be called a *son* of the *Father* and a *member* of *Christ*.

*Fourthly*, he should earnestly consider that *wrathful death* awaits him every hour and moment, and will lay hold of him in his *sins* in his *garment of a swine-herd*, and throw him into the *pit of Hell* as a *perjured person* and *breaker of faith*, who deserves to be kept in the dark dungeon of death until the *Judgment* of GOD.

*Fifthly*, he should consider the earnest and severe *Day* of GOD'S *Final Judgment*, when he shall be presented living *with his abominations* before GOD'S *tribunal*. All those whom he has here offended or injured by words and works, and caused to do evil (so that by his instigation or compulsion they also have committed evil), shall come against him, cursing him before the eyes of *Christ* and of all holy angels and people. There he shall stand in great *shame* and *disgrace*, and also in great *terror* and *despair*; and it shall forever grieve him to reflect that he has trifled away such a *glorious* and eternal state of salvation and happiness for the *pleasure* of such a *short* time; and that he had not taken care in that *short* time to secure for himself a share in the *communion* of the *saints*, and so to have enjoyed with them *eternal light* and *divine glory*.

*Sixthly*, he must consider that the ungodly man loses his noble *image*, GOD having created him in and for His *image* or representation as a creature, and gets instead a *deformed* or *monstrous shape*, like a *hellish worm* or *ugly beast*. In this state he is an enemy to GOD, Heaven, and all holy angels and people, and his communion is, and will be forever, with the *devils* and *hellish worms* in horrible *darkness*.

*Seventhly*, he must earnestly consider the *eternal punishment* and *torture* of the *damned*; how in eternal horror they shall suffer *torments* in their *abominations* which they had com-

7. All this a man must earnestly and seriously consider, and remember also that GOD had originally created him in such a fair and glorious *image*, even in *His own likeness*, in which *He Himself* would dwell. That He created him out of His goodness for man’s own eternal bliss and glory, to the end that he might dwell with the holy *angels* and *children of GOD* in great *happiness, power, and glory*; in the eternal *light*; in the praiseful and melodious *harmony* of the *angelical* and *divine kingdom* of *joy*. Where he should rejoice continually with the *children of GOD* without fear of any end. Where no *evil thoughts* could touch him, neither *care* nor *trouble*, neither *heat* nor *cold*. Where no *night* is known; where there is no *day* or limited *time* anymore, but an *everlasting blessedness*, wherein soul and body *tremble for joy*. And where he himself should rejoice at the infinite wonders and virtues appearing in the brightness of colors, and the variety of splendor opened and displayed by the omnipotent powers and glories of GOD, upon the new *crystalline earth*, which shall be as transparent *glass*. And that he does so willfully lose all this *eternal glory* and happiness for the sake of so short and poor a *time*, which even in this state of *vanity and corruption*, in the evil life of the voluptuous flesh, is full of *misery, fear, and utter vexation*; and wherein it goes with the wicked as with the righteous—as the one must die, so must the other; only the death of the saints is an entrance into the eternal *rest*, while the death of the wicked is an introduction into the eternal *anguish*.

*Eighthly*, he must consider the *course of this world*, that all things in it are but a *play*, with which he spends his time in such unquietness; and that it goes with the *rich* and *mighty* as with the *poor* and the *beggar*. That all of us equally live and move in the four elements; and that the hard-earned morsel of the *poor* is as relishing and savory to him in his *labor*, as the dainties of the *rich* are to him in his *cares*. Also, that all of us subsist by one breath, and that the *rich man* has nothing but the *pleasures of the palate* and the *lust of the eye* for a little while, more than his poor neighbor, for the end of both is the same. Yet for this short-lived *lust’s sake*, many foolishly forego inconceivable happiness, and bring themselves into such extreme and eternal misery.

In the deep consideration of these weighty truths, man shall come to *feel* in his *heart* and *mind*, especially if he at the same time represents and sets before his eyes *his own end*, a hearty *sighing* and *longing* after the *mercy of GOD*, and will begin to

He will then first begin to find in himself that he has not yet labored at all in the *vineyard of Christ*, but that he is a *dry, fruitless branch of the vine*. And thus in many a person, whom the *Spirit of Christ* touches in such a consideration, there arises abundant *sorrow*, *grief of heart*, and inward *lamentation* over the days of his wickedness which he has spent in vanity, without any working in the *vineyard of Christ*.

Such a man whom the *Spirit of Christ* thus brings into *sorrow and repentance*, so that his heart is opened both to know and bewail his sins, is very easily helped. He needs only to draw to himself the *promise of Christ*, namely, *that God does not will the death of a sinner, but that He wishes them all to come to Him, and He will refresh them*; and that *there is great joy in heaven for one sinner who repents*. Let such a person but lay hold on the *words of Christ*, and wrap himself up in His meritorious *passion and death*.

But I will now speak to those who feel indeed in themselves a *desire* to repent, and yet cannot come to *acknowledge* and *bewail* their committed sins. The *flesh* says continually to the soul, "*stay a while, it is well enough*"; or "*it is time enough tomorrow*"; and when tomorrow has come, then the *flesh* says again "*tomorrow*"; the soul in the meanwhile, sighing and fainting, conceives neither any true *sorrow* for the sins it has committed, nor any *comfort*. To such a person, I say, I will write a *process* or *way*, which I myself have gone, so that he may know what he must do, and how it went with me, if perhaps he is inclined to enter into and pursue the same, and then he will come to understand what he shall find here afterwards written.

## A Process of Repentance;
### Or Way to Conversion.

<meta>This page continues the guidance for a soul seeking true repentance, specifically addressing those who feel a desire to repent but struggle with the distractions of the flesh and the world, providing a practical method for committing to a spiritual transformation.</meta>

### Of True Repentance.

A soul that continually sighs, fears, and must necessarily acknowledge itself guilty of sins before the *Judgment of God*—such a person, I say, can take no better course than this: namely, to bind his *senses, mind,* and *reason* together and immediately create—as soon as he perceives in himself the desire to repent—a mighty, strong *purpose* and *resolution* that he will *that very hour, nay that minute,* immediately enter into *repentance*. He must go out from his wicked way and not regard the *power* and *status* of the *world* at all. Indeed, even if it were required, he would forsake and disregard all things for the sake of true *repentance*, and never depart from that *resolution* again, even if he should be made a *fool* and the *scorn* of all the *world* for it.

Instead, with the full *intent* and *strength* of his *mind*, he will go out from the *beauty* and *pleasure* of the *world* and patiently enter into the *passion* and *death of Christ* in and under the *cross*, setting all his *hope* and *confidence* upon the *life* to come. From this moment on, in *righteousness* and *truth*, he will enter into the *vineyard of Christ* and do the *will* of *God*. In the *spirit* and *will of Christ*, he will begin and finish all his *actions* in this *world*. For the sake of *Christ's word* and *promise*, which offers us a *heavenly reward*, he will willingly take up and bear every *adversity* and *cross*, so that he may be admitted into the *communion* or *fellowship* of the *children of Christ* and be incorporated and united to *His humanity* in the *blood of the Lamb, Jesus Christ*.

He must firmly conceive within himself, and wholly envelop his *soul* in this *conviction*: that in such a *purpose*, he shall obtain the *love of God* in *Christ Jesus*, and that *God* will give to him, according to *His faithful promise*, that noble *pledge, the Holy Spirit*, as an <term>earnest</term> <note>A down payment or spiritual guarantee.</note>. He must believe that, in the *humanity of Christ* regarding the *heavenly substance*, he shall be born again within himself, and that the *spirit of Christ* will renew his *mind* with *His love* and *power* and strengthen his weak *faith*. Also, he must believe that in his *divine hunger*, he shall receive the *flesh* and *blood of Christ* for *food* and *drink* in the *desire of his soul*, which hungers and thirsts after it as its proper *nourishment*; and that with the *thirst of the soul*, he will drink the *water of eternal life* out of the sweet *fountain of Jesus Christ*, just as *Christ's* most true and steadfast *promise* is.

He must also wholly and firmly imagine to himself, and set before him, the *great love of God*: that *God* does not will the *death of a sinner*, but rather that *he repent and believe*; that *Christ* calls poor *sinners* very kindly and graciously to Himself and will *refresh them*; that "*God has sent His Son into the world to seek and save that which is lost*," namely, the poor *repentant* and returning *sinner*; and that for the poor *sinner's* sake

<summary>This page advises the soul to make an immediate, firm resolution to repent, forsaking worldly pleasures and status to enter into the "vineyard of Christ." It emphasizes that through this commitment, the believer receives the Holy Spirit and spiritual nourishment through union with Christ.</summary>

<header>The Way to Christ.</header>

He has given *His Life* unto *Death*, and died for him in our *Humanity* which *He* took upon *Himself*.

Furthermore, he must firmly persuade himself that GOD in *Christ Jesus* will much more readily hear him and receive him to *Grace* than he will come to Him; and that GOD in the Love of *Christ*, in the most dear and precious Name JESUS, *cannot will any Evil*. There is no *angry* countenance at all in *this Name*, but it is the *highest* and *deepest Love* and *Faithfulness*, the *greatest Sweetness* of the Deity, in the great Name JEHOVAH, which He has manifested in our *Humanity*—corrupted as it is, and perished as to the *Heavenly Part*, which in *Paradise* disappeared through *Sin*. And He was therefore moved in His *Heart* to flow into us with His *sweet Love*, so that the *Anger* of His *Father*, which was kindled in us, might be quenched and turned into *Love* by it. All this was done for the poor sinner’s *sake*, so that he might obtain an open *gate* of *grace* again.

In this consideration, he must firmly imagine to himself that this very hour and instant he stands before the face of the *Holy Trinity*, and that GOD is really present within and without him, as the *Holy Scripture* witnesses, saying,
> "Am I not He who fills all things?"

And in another place,
> "*The Word* is near you, in your mouth, and in your heart." — Also, "We will come to you, and make our abode with you."

And,
> "Behold, I am with you always, even to the end of the world."

And again,
> "The Kingdom of God is within you."

Thus he must firmly know and believe that inwardly he stands really before the face of *Jesus Christ*, even before the *Holy Deity*, on whom his *soul* has turned its *back*; and he must resolve that he will this very hour turn the *eyes* and *desire* of his *soul* toward GOD again, and with the poor *lost* and *returning son* come to the *Father*. He must, with the *eyes* of his *mind* cast down in fear and deepest *humility*, begin to *confess his sins* and *unworthiness* before the face of GOD in the *manner* following.

## A Short Form of Confession before the Face of GOD.

*Everyone, as his case and necessity require, may order and enlarge this confession as the Holy Spirit shall teach him. I will only set down a short direction.*

> O YOU great unsearchable GOD, LORD of all things; You, who in *Christ Jesus*, out of great *love* toward us, have manifested yourself with your *holy substance* in our humanity: I, a poor unworthy sinful wretch, come before your presence, which you have manifested in the humanity of *Jesus Christ*, though I am not worthy to lift up my eyes to you, acknowledging and confessing before you, that I am guilty of unfaithfulness, and breaking off from your great *love* and *grace*, which you have freely bestowed upon us. I have left the *covenant*, which out of mere grace you made with me in *baptism*, in which you received me to be a child and heir of eternal life, and have brought my *desire* into the vanity of this world, and defiled my soul with it, and made it altogether *bestial* and *earthly*. So that my soul knows not itself, because of the *mire of sin*; but considers itself a strange child before your face, not worthy to desire your grace. I lie in the *guilt* and *filth of sin*, and the vanity of my corrupt flesh, up to the very lips of my soul, and have but a *small spark* of the *living breath* left in me, which desires your grace. I am dead in sin and corruption, so that in this woeful condition I dare not lift up my eyes to you.
>
> O GOD in *Christ Jesus*, you who for poor sinners’ sakes *became man* to help them, to you I complain, in you I have yet a *spark of refuge* in my soul. I have not regarded your purchased *inheritance*, which you purchased for us poor men by your *bitter death*, but have made myself a partaker of the heritage of vanity, in the *anger* of my Father in the curse of the earth, and am ensensnared in sin, and nearly dead as to your kingdom. I lie in feebleness as to your strength, and the *wrathful death* waits for me. The devil has poisoned me, so that I do not know my Savior: I have become a *wild branch* on your *tree*, and have consumed my *inheritance* which is in you, with the *devil’s swine*. What shall I say before you, who am not worthy of your grace? I lie in the *sleep of death* which has captivated me, and am fast bound with <term>three strong chains</term>. O

Now, in myself, I have become<gloss>c</gloss> a fool; I am *naked* and *bare*; my *shame* stands before my eyes; I cannot hide it; your judgment waits for me. What shall I say before you, who are the judge of all the world? I have nothing to bring before you.—Here I stand *naked* and *bare* in your presence, and fall down before your face, bewailing my misery and flying to your great mercy, though I am not worthy of it; yet receive me but in *your death*, and let me but die from my death in *yours*. I pray you, cast me down to the ground in my innate *self*, and kill this *self* of mine through your *death*, so that I may live no more for *myself*, seeing that I in *myself* work nothing but *sin*. Therefore, I pray you, cast down to the ground this wicked *beast* which is full of false *deceit* and *self-desire*, and deliver this poor soul of mine from its heavy bonds.

O merciful GOD, it is owing to your love and patience that I do not already lie in hell. I surrender myself, with my whole *will*, *senses*, and *mind*, to your grace, and fly to your mercy. I call upon you through *your death*, from that small spark of *life* in me surrounded by *death* and *hell*, which open their throats against me and would wholly swallow me up in *death*; I call upon you, who have promised that *you will not snuff out the smoldering wick*. I have no other way to you but by *your own bitter death and passion*, because you have made our death life by your *humanity*, and broken the chains of *death*; and therefore I sink the desire of my soul down into *your death*, into the gate of *your death* which you have broken open.

O you great fountain of the *love* of GOD, I beg you, help me, so that I may die from my vanity and sin in the *death of my Redeemer, Jesus Christ*.

O you breath of the great *love* of GOD, I beg you, quicken my weak breath in me, so that it may begin to *hunger* and *thirst* after you. Oh, how extremely faint I am for want of your strength! O merciful GOD, I beg you, turn me; I cannot turn myself. O you *Vanquisher of death*, I pray you, help me to wrestle. # The Way to Christ.

Your promise, which you have made, saying, "As I live, I do not desire the death of a sinner, but that he should turn and live." I sink myself down into the *death* of my *Redeemer Jesus Christ*, and wait for you, whose word is truth and life. *Amen.*

---

In this, or a similar way, everyone may confess *his sins*, as he himself finds upon examining his conscience—what sins he has brought his soul into. Yet if his *purpose* is *truly earnest*, using a form is unnecessary, for the *Spirit of God*, which at that moment is in the *will of the mind*, will itself make the prayer for him within his *interior*. For it is the *Spirit of God* which, in a true and earnest *desire*, works repentance and *intercedes for the soul before God*, through the *death of Christ*.

But I will not hide from the beloved reader who has a *Christian intent* how it commonly goes with those who are in such a firm *purpose* and *resolution*. Though indeed it goes differently for one than for another, according to whether his *purpose* is more or less earnest and *strong*. For the *Spirit of God is not bound*, but uses various ways or processes according to what he knows to be fittest for everyone. Yet a soldier who has been in the wars knows how to fight and can instruct another who may happen to be in a similar condition.

Now when such a heart with *strong resolution* and *purpose* does thus come before God and enter into *repentance*, it happens to it as it did to the *Canaanitish woman*; that is, it seems as if God will not hear. *The heart remains without comfort*; its sins, follies, and neglects also present themselves before it and make it *feel* unworthy of any. The mind is, as it were, *speechless*; the soul *groans* in the depths; the heart receives nothing, nor can it even pour forth its *confession* before God; instead, it is as if the heart and soul were completely shut up. The soul would gladly go toward God, but the flesh keeps it captive. The Devil also shuts it up tightly and presents the way of vanity to it again, tickling it with the lusts of the flesh, and saying inwardly to it, "Stay a while; do this or that first; get enough money or goods beforehand so that you do not need to depend on the world, and afterwards enter into *repentance* and a holy life; there will be time enough then."

Oh, how many hundreds perish in such a beginning if they go back again into vanity and become like young *grafts* broken off by the wind or withered by the heat!

Beloved soul, take note: if you will be a *champion* in your Savior *Christ* against death and hell, and if you would have your *young graft* grow and become a *tree* in the kingdom of *Christ*, you must go on and stand fast in your first *earnest purpose*. It is worth as much as your paternal inheritance and your body—

<summary>The author explains that true repentance is an internal work of the Holy Spirit rather than a fixed formula and warns that seekers often face spiritual silence and demonic temptations to return to worldly vanities.</summary>

<keywords>repentance, Spirit of God, prayer, temptation, Canaanitish woman, spiritual struggle, persistence, vanity</keywords>

--- Page 40 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the process of true repentance, following the description of the soul's initial struggle and its need to remain steadfast despite feeling unworthy or unheard by God.</meta>

and *soul* too, whether you become an *angel* in God, or a *devil* in hell. If you will be crowned, you must fight; you must overcome in *Christ*, and not yield to the *devil*. Your purpose must stand firm; you must not prefer temporal honor and goods before it. When the *spirit of the flesh* says, "Stay a while, it is not convenient yet," then the soul must say, "Now is the time for me to go back again into my native country, out of which my father *Adam* has brought me. No creature shall keep me back; and though you, earthly body, should thereby decay and perish, yet I will now enter with my will and whole desire into the rose garden of my Redeemer *Jesus Christ*, through his suffering and death into him, and in the death of *Christ* subdue you, you earthly body, that has swallowed up my *pearl* from me, which God gave to my father *Adam* in *paradise*. I will break the *will* of your *voluptuousness*, which is in *vanity*, and bind you like a *mad dog* with the *chain* of my earnest purpose; and though you should thereby become a *fool* in the account of all *men*, yet you must and shall obey the firm *resolve* of my *soul*. Nothing shall unloose you from this *chain* but *temporal death*. May God and his *strength* help me in this."

## A Short Direction

How the poor *soul* must come before *God* again, and how it must fight for the noble *garland*; what kind of *weapons* it must use, if it will go to *war* against *God’s anger*, against the *devil*, the *world* and *sin*, against *flesh* and *blood*, against the *influence* of the *stars* and *elements*, and all its other *enemies*.

BELOVED *soul*, there is *earnestness* required to do this, and not a bare *recital of words* only! No, the *earnest resolved will* must drive on this *work*, otherwise nothing will be effected. For if the *soul* will obtain the *triumphal garland* of *Christ* from the *noble Sophia*, or *Divine Wisdom*, it must woo her for it in great *desire of love*. It must entreat her in her most *holy name* for it, and come before her in most *modest humility*, and not like a *lustful bull* or a *wanton Venus*. For so long as any are such, they must not seek these *things*; for they shall not obtain them, and though something should be obtained by those who are in such an *impure state*, it would be no more than a *glimpse of the true glory*. But a *chaste* and *modest mind*

<summary>The author describes the soul's necessary resolve to subdue the earthly body and its vanities to return to God, introducing instructions on the spiritual warfare required to win the "triumphal garland" from Divine Wisdom (Sophia).</summary>
<keywords>repentance, soul, spiritual warfare, Adam, Sophia, Divine Wisdom, flesh vs spirit, Jesus Christ, resolve</keywords>

--- Page 41 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for the soul’s return to God, specifically addressing the temporary nature of the "Heavenly Garland" and the total commitment required to enter the "Way to Christ."</meta>

...may prevail so far as to have the soul in its noble image, which died in *Adam*, quickened in the *heavenly corporality* as to the *inward ground*, and the precious *garland* set upon it. Yet if this should come to pass, it is taken off again from the soul and laid aside, as a *crown* is usually done after a king is once crowned with it; it is then laid aside and kept. So it also happens with the *heavenly garland* or *gift*. It is taken from the soul again because the soul is still encompassed with the *house of sin*; so that if it should unhappily fall again, its *crown* might not be *defiled*. This is spoken plainly enough for the children who know and have tried these things: None of the wicked are worthy to know more about them.

---

## The Process, or Way.

A PERSON must bring a serious *mind* to this *work*. He must come before GOD with *sincere earnestness*, *deep humility*, and *hearty sorrow for his sins*, and with a *deliberate* and *firm resolution* not to enter any more into the old *broad way of vanity*. And though the whole world should consider him a fool, and he should lose both honor and goods—no, and the temporal life also—for the sake of his *new choice*, yet he must resolve firmly to abide by it.

If he will ever obtain the *love* and *marriage* of the noble *Sophia*, he must make such a *vow* as this in his *purpose* and *mind*. For *Christ* himself says: 

> "He that does not forsake wife and children,
> brothers and sisters, money and goods, and all that he has,
> and even his earthly life also, to follow me, is not worthy of
> me."

Here *Christ* means the *mind* of the *soul*; so that if there were anything that would keep the *mind* back from it, though it should have ever so fair and glorious a pretense or show in this world, the *mind* must not regard it at all, but rather part with it than with the *love* of the *noble virgin Sophia*, in the *bud* and *blossom* of *Christ*, in His tender *humanity* in us as to the *heavenly corporality*. For this is the *flower* in *Sharon*, the *rose* in the *valley* of *Jericho*, wherewith *Solomon* delighted himself, and called it his *dear love*, his *chaste virgin* whom he loved; as indeed all other saints before and after him did; whoever obtained her called her his *pearl*.

Regarding the manner in which to pray for it, you may see by this short direction following. The work itself must be committed to the *Holy Spirit*; He forms and frames the prayer for the soul in every heart where He is sought.

## The Prayer.

I, a poor, unworthy creature, come before you, O great and *holy GOD*, and lift up my eyes to you. Although I am not worthy, yet your great mercy and your faithful promise in your *Word* have *now* encouraged me to lift the eyes of the *desire* of my *soul* up to you. For my *soul* has *now* laid hold of the *Word* of your promise, and received it into itself, and comes to you with it. And though it is but an *estranged child* which was disobedient to you, yet *now* it desires to be obedient; and it *now* enfolds itself with its *desire* into that *Word* which became *Man*, which became *flesh and blood*, and has broken sin and death in my *humanity*. This has changed the *anger* of *GOD* into *love* in the *soul*, has deprived *death* of his power, and *hell* of its victory over *soul and body*; and it has opened a *gate* for my *soul* to the clear *face* of your *strength and power*. O *great* and most *holy GOD*, I have brought the *hunger and desire* of my *soul* into this most *holy Word*, and *now I come* before you, and in my *hunger* call to you, *you living fountain*, through your *Word* which became *flesh and blood*. *Your Word* being made the *life* in our *flesh*, I receive it firmly into the *desire* of my *soul* as my own *life*; and I reach into you with the *desire* of my *soul* through the *Word* in the *flesh* of *Christ*; through his *holy conception* in the *Virgin Mary*, his *incarnation*, his *holy nativity*, his *baptism* in *Jordan*, his *temptation* in the *wilderness*, where *he* overcame the *kingdom* of the *devil* and this *world* in his *humanity*. Through all his *miracles*, which *he* performed on *earth*; through his *reproach* and *shame*, his *innocent death and passion*, the *shedding* of his *blood*, wherein *GOD'S anger* in *soul and flesh* was drowned. Through his *rest* in the *tomb*, when *he* awakened our *father Adam* out of *his sleep*, who had fallen into a *dead sleep* regarding the *kingdom* of *heaven*. Through his *love*, which pierced through the *anger* and destroyed *hell* in the *soul*. Through his *resurrection* from the *dead*, his *ascension*, the *sending* of the *Holy Spirit* into our *soul and spirit*, and through all his *promises*; one of which is 'that you, O *GOD* the *Father*, will give the *Holy Spirit* to those 'who ask for it, in the *name* and through the *Word* which became ' *man*.

O you *life* of my *flesh* and of my *soul* in *Christ my brother*, I beseech you in the *hunger* of my *soul*, and entreat you with all my *powers*, though they are weak, to give me what you have promised me, and freely bestow upon me in my *Saviour Jesus*

The Way to Christ.
18

> *Christ*, His *Flesh* for *Food* and his *Blood* for *Drink*, to refresh my poor hungry Soul, so that it may be quickened and strengthened in the *Word* which became Man, by which it may long and hunger after you correctly.

> O you deepest Love in the most sweet Name JESUS, give yourself into the Desire of my Soul. For this reason you have moved yourself, and according to your great Sweetness manifested yourself in Human Nature, and called us to you: *we who hunger and thirst after you, and you have promised us that you will refresh us.* I now open the Lips of my Soul to you, O you sweet Truth; and though I am not worthy to desire it of your Holiness, yet I come to you through your bitter *Passion* and *Death*: you having sprinkled my Uncleanness with your *Blood*, and sanctified me in your *Humanity*, and made an open Gate for me through *your Death* to your sweet Love in your *Blood*. Through your five holy Wounds, from which you shed your *Blood*, I bring the Desire of my Soul into your Love.

> O *Jesus Christ*, you Son of GOD and Man, I pray you receive into yourself your purchased Inheritance, which your Father has given you. I cry within me, that I may enter through your holy *Blood* and *Death* into you. Open yourself in me, so that the Spirit of my Soul may reach you and receive you into it. Lay hold of my Thirst in me with your Thirst; bring your Thirst after us humans, which you had upon the Cross, into my Thirst, and give me your *Blood* to drink in my Thirst. So that my death in me, which holds me captive, may be drowned in the *Blood* of your Love, and that my extinguished or suppressed Image—which, as to the Kingdom of Heaven, disappeared in my father *Adam* through sin—may be made alive through your powerful *Blood*, and my Soul clothed with it again as with the new Body which dwells in Heaven. In this Image, your holy Power and *Word* that became Man dwells, which is the *Temple of the Holy Spirit*, who dwells in us according to your Promise, saying, "we will come to you, and make our Abode with you."

> O you great Love of *Jesus Christ*, I can do no more than sink my *Desire* into you; your *Word* which became Man is Truth; since you have bidden me come, now I come. May it be done to me according to your Word and Will. *Amen*.

<header>Of True Repentance.</header>

# A Warning to the Reader.

BELOVED Reader, out of love for you, I will not hide from you what is here earnestly shown to me. If you still love the *Vanity* of the *Flesh*, and are not sincerely intending to follow the way to the *New Birth* of *Regeneration*, aiming to become a *New Man*, then leave the prayers written above unspoken; otherwise they will turn to a *Judgment* of GOD within you. You must not take the holy names in vain; you are faithfully warned: they belong to the *Thirsty Soul*. But if your soul is in earnest, it shall find by experience what words they are.

---

# A Direction,

### *How the Soul must meet its Beloved, when she knocks at its Centre, or Closed Inner-Chamber.*

BELOVED Soul, if you will be earnest without ceasing, you shall certainly obtain the favor of a *Kiss* from the *Noble Sophia* (*or Divine Wisdom*) in the Holy Name JESUS; for she stands always before the door of the *Soul*, *knocking*, and warning the sinner of his wicked way. Now if he once desires her love in this way, she is ready for him, and *kisses* him with the beams of her sweet love, from which the heart receives joy. But she does not immediately unite herself with the soul; that is, she does not immediately awaken the *extinguished Heavenly Image* in herself, which disappeared in *Adam* in *Paradise*. No, there might be danger to man in that; for if *Adam* and *Lucifer* fell while having it manifested in them, the same might easily happen to a person who is still so strongly enslaved to vanity.

The bond of your promise must be firm and steadfast. Before she will *Crown* you, you must be tempted and tried: she takes the beams of her love from you again to see whether you will prove faithful; she also lets you stand at a distance, as it were, and does not answer you even with one look of her love. For before she will *Crown* you, you must be *judged*, so that you may taste the bitter *Potion of Dregs*, which you have filled for yourself in your abominations. You must come before the gates of *Hell* first, and there demonstrate your victory for her in her love, in that strength with which she supported you against the *Devil’s* malign influence.

<language>English</language>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<page-num>20</page-num>
<header>The Way to Christ.</header>

*Christ* was tempted in the Wilderness, and if you wish to put Him on, you must go *through* His whole progress or journey, even from His Incarnation to His Ascension. And though you are not able, nor required, to do what He has done; yet you must enter wholly into His *process*, and therein die continually from the *corruption* of the soul. For the *Virgin Sophia* does not marry herself to the soul, except in this nature which springs up in the soul through the *death of Christ*, like a new plant standing in Heaven. The *earthly body* cannot understand her during its lifetime, for it must first die to its corruptible vanity; but the *Heavenly Image* which disappeared in *Adam*—namely, the true *seed of the woman* in which GOD became man, and into which He brought His living seed, the *Heavenly Substantiality*—is capable of receiving the *Pearl*, in the same way it happened to *Mary*, at the *end* or *fulfillment* of the *covenant*.

Therefore, take heed what you do; when you have made your promise, keep it; and then she will *crown* you more readily than you would want to be crowned. But you must be certain, when the Tempter comes to you with the pleasure, beauty, and glory of the world, that your mind rejects it and says,

> ‘ I must be a servant
> ‘ and not a master in the vineyard of Christ; I am but a steward
> ‘ of God in and over all that I have, and I must do with it as His
> ‘ Word teaches me; my heart must sit down with the simple
> ‘ and lowly, in the dust, and be always humble.’

*Whatever* state and condition you are in, *humility* must be at the front, or else you will not obtain the noble *Virgin* in *marriage*. The free will of your soul must withstand the attack like a *champion*; for if the *Devil* cannot prevail against the soul with vanity, nor catch it with that bait, then he comes with its *unworthiness and catalogue of sins*. And there you must fight hard, and the *merits of Christ* must be set at the front, or else the creature cannot prevail against the *Devil*. For in this conflict it goes so terribly with many a poor sinner, that *outward reason thinks* him to be distracted, or possessed by an evil *spirit*. The *Devil* defends himself so horribly in some—especially if he has had a great *stronghold of prey* in them—that he must be stoutly assaulted before he will depart and leave his castle. In this kind of combat *Heaven* and *Hell* are fighting one against the other.

Now if the soul remains constant, and gains the victory over the *Devil* in *all* his assaults, disregarding all temporal things for the love of its *noble Sophia*, then the *precious garland* will be set upon it as a token or sign of victory.

Here the *Virgin* (who reveals herself in the dear name of *JESUS CHRIST*, the *Treader upon the Serpent*, *God’s Anointed*) comes to the soul, and kisses it with her sweetest

<summary>This page explains that the soul must undergo a spiritual "process" modeled after Christ's life, involving a total rejection of worldly vanity and an intense battle against the Devil's temptations. Only through constant humility and the merits of Christ can the soul overcome these assaults to receive the crown of victory and the kiss of Divine Wisdom (Sophia).</summary>

Love in its most inward essence impresses her *Love* into its *Desire* as a token of victory. And here, the *heavenly part* of *Adam* rises again from death in *Christ*. I cannot write about this, for there is no pen in this world that can express it! It is the *Wedding of the Lamb*, where the noble *Pearl* is sown with very great triumph, though in the *beginning* it is small, like a *grain of mustard seed*, as *Christ* says.

Now when the *wedding* is over, the soul must take heed that this *Pearl-Tree* or *Tree of Faith* sprouts and grows, as it has promised the *Virgin*. For then the *Devil* will immediately come with his furious storm—the ungodly people, who will scoff at, despise, and denounce this way as *madness*; and then a person must enter into the process of *Christ*, under His *cross*. Here it will appear indeed and in truth what sort of a *Christian* he is. For he must allow himself to be proclaimed a *fool* and an ungodly *wretch*; even his greatest *friends*, who favored him or flattered him in the lusts of the flesh, will now be his *enemies*, and though they do not know why, they will *hate* him. Thus it is that *Christ* hides His *bride* entirely under the *cross*, so that she may not be known in this world; *The Devil* also strives so that these children may be hidden from the *world*, lest perhaps many such *branches* should grow in that garden which he supposes to be his.

This I have set down for the information of the Christian-minded reader, so that he may know what to do if the same should happen to him.

Into you I sink down in the *Anguish* of my *Conscience*; do with me what you will. I fly to you, you righteous Judge, through the *Anguish* of my Redeemer *Jesus Christ*, when he sweated the bloody sweat on the *Mount* of *Olives* for my sake, and was scourged by *Pontius Pilate* for me, and suffered a *Crown* of *Thorns* to be pressed upon his head, so that his *Blood* came forth.

You have freely given me *his Anguish* and drowned your *Wrath* in *him*. And though I have not accepted it, but have departed from him and become faithless, yet you have given me this precious pledge in my *Flesh* and *Blood*. For he has taken my *Flesh* and *Soul* upon *his Heavenly Flesh* and *Blood*, and has satisfied the *Anger* in my flesh and soul in him, with *his heavenly Blood*. Therefore receive me now in his *Satisfaction*, and put his *Anguish, Passion,* and *Death* against your *Wrath*, which is kindled in me, and break your *Judgment* in me through the *Blood* of *his Love*.

O great *Love*! In the *Blood* and *Death* of *Jesus Christ*, I beseech you to break the strong *Fort* of *Prey* which the *Devil* has made and built up in me, where he resists me in the way of your *Grace*. Drive him out of me, so that he may not overcome me; for no one living can stand in your sight if you withdraw your hand from them.

O come, you *Breaker-through* the *Anger* of GOD, destroy its power, and help my poor soul to fight and overcome it. O bring me into *Victory*, and uphold me in you; break into pieces its seat in my *Vanity*, which is kindled in my soul and flesh.

This I lay hold of, and let the *anger* of GOD do what it will with my sin, and let the *devil* roar over my soul in his *fort of prey* which he has made, as much as he will: Neither the *devil, death,* nor *hell* shall pull me out of my *Saviour’s wounds*. You must at length be confounded in me, you malicious *devil*, and your *fort of prey* must be forsaken, for I will drown it in the *love of Jesus Christ*, and then dwell in it if you can. *Amen.*

---

## Information regarding Temptation.

Beloved reader, this is no *jesting matter*; he who accounts it so has not tried it, nor has he yet passed the *judgment*; but his *conscience* is still *asleep*. And though it should be deferred to his later days, which is *very dangerous*, yet he must pass through this *judgment*, or *fiery trial*. Happy is he who passes through it in the time of his *youth*, before the *devil* builds his *fort of prey* strong; he may afterwards prove a *labourer* in the *heavenly vineyard*, and sow his *seed* in the *garden of Christ*, where he shall reap the *fruit* in due time. This *judgment* continues a long while upon many a poor soul—several years, if he does not earnestly and *early* put on the *armour of Christ*, but waits until the *judgment of tribulation* first drives him to *repentance*. But to him who comes of himself, of his own *earnest purpose*, and endeavors to depart from his evil ways, the temptation or trial will not be so *hard*, nor will it continue so *long*. Yet he must stand out *valiantly* until victory is gained over the *devil*; for he shall be *mightily assisted*, and all shall end in the *best* for him. So that afterwards, when the *day breaks* in his soul, he turns it to the great praise and glory of GOD, that his grand enemy and persecutor was overcome in the conflict.

<summary>The text concludes a prayer for deliverance through Christ's blood and begins an instruction on the necessity of spiritual trials. It advises that undergoing repentance and the "fiery trial" early in life is less difficult than waiting until driven by tribulation.</summary>
<keywords>Repentance, Temptation, Conscience, Judgment, Fort of Prey, Armour of Christ, Spiritual Trial, Victory</keywords>
