# OF THE
# SUPER-SENSUAL LIFE,

->OR THE<-

## *Life which is above the Senses.*

->In a Dialogue between a *Scholar* or *Disciple* and his *Master*<-

1. THE Scholar said to his Master; How may I come to the *super-sensual life*, so that I may see God and *hear* him speak?
His Master said, When you can throw yourself for a moment into *that* where no *creature* dwells, then you *hear* what God speaks.
2. THE SCHOLAR.—Is *that* nearby, or far off?
THE MASTER.—It is *within* you, and if you can for a while cease from all *your thinking* and *willing*, you shall *hear* the unspeakable words of God.
3. THE SCHOLAR.—How can I *hear* when I stand still from *thinking* and *willing*?
THE MASTER.—When you stand still from the *thinking* and *willing* of *self*, then the *eternal hearing, seeing,* and *speaking* will be revealed *within* you; and so God hears and sees through you. *Your own* hearing, willing, and seeing hinders you, so that you do not *see* nor *hear* God.
4. THE SCHOLAR.—*With what* shall I *hear* and *see* God, seeing he is above *nature* and *creature*?
THE MASTER.—When you are *quiet* or *silent*, then you are *that* which God was before *nature* and *creature*, and from which he made your *nature* and *creature*; then you *hear* and *see* with *that* with which God saw and heard *within* you before *your own* willing, seeing, and hearing began.
5. THE SCHOLAR.—What hinders or keeps me back so that I cannot come to *that*?
THE MASTER.—*Your own* willing, hearing, and seeing; and because you strive against *that* from which you have come, you break yourself off with *your own* willing from *God's willing*, and with *your own* seeing you see in *your own* willing only, and your willing stops your hearing with *your own* thinking of

<summary>A scholar asks his master how to attain a spiritual life that transcends the physical senses to see and hear God. The master explains that one must quiet the self’s own thoughts and will to allow God’s eternal presence and voice to be revealed from within.</summary>
<keywords>super-sensual life, spiritual hearing, self-will, stillness, divine revelation, nature and creature, mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue of "The Super-sensual Life," focusing on the requirements for reaching the supernatural ground and the nature of true dominion over the world.</meta>

<page-num>96</page-num>
<header>The Way to Christ.</header>

*earthly natural things*, and brings you into a *ground of essential state*, and darkens you with *that* which you *will*, so that you cannot come to *that* which is *supernatural* and *super-sensual*.

6. THE SCHOLAR.—Seeing I am in *nature*, how may I come through *nature* into the *super-sensual ground*, without destroying *nature*?

THE MASTER.—*Three things* are requisite here; the first is, you must *resign your will to God*, and sink yourself down to the ground in his *mercy*. The second is, you must *hate your own will*, and not do that to which your *own will* drives you. The *third* is, you must *submit yourself under the Cross*, that you may be able to bear the temptations of *nature* and *creature*. If you do so, God will speak *into* you, and bring your resigned *will into himself*, into the *supernatural ground*; and then you shall *hear what the Lord speaks in you*.

7. THE SCHOLAR.—If I should do thus, I must forsake the *world* and my *life* too.

THE MASTER.—If you forsake the *world*, you come into *that* of which the *world* is made; and if you lose *your life*, and come to have *your own power* faint, then your *life* is in *that* for whose sake you forsake it, *namely*, in God, from whom it came into the body.

8. THE SCHOLAR.—God has created man in and for the natural life, to rule over all *creatures* on Earth, and to be a *lord* over all things in this world; and therefore *reason* suggests that he should possess it as *his own*.

---

### *How men may and should rule over all creatures, and can be like all things.*

THE MASTER.—If you rule over all creatures *outwardly* only, then your *will* and *ruling* is in a *bestial kind*, and is but an *imaginary transitory ruling*; and you also bring your *desire* into a *bestial essence*, by which you become infected and captivated, and get also a *bestial condition*. But if you have left the *imaginary condition*, then you are in the *super-imaginariness*, and rule over all *creatures* in that *ground* out of which they are created, and nothing on Earth can hurt you; for you are *like* all things, and nothing is unlike to you.

9. THE SCHOLAR.—O loving Master, pray teach me the shortest way *how* I may come to be *like all things*.

THE MASTER.—With all my heart; only think on the words

<summary>The Master explains that reaching the super-sensual state requires resigning one's will to God and submitting to the Cross. He further describes how true mastery over the world comes not from outward bestial rule, but from a state of "super-imaginariness" where one is like all things.</summary>

of our *Lord Jesus Christ*, when he said, ‘Unless you turn and become as little children, you shall not see the Kingdom of God.’ Now if you will be *like* all things, you must *forsake* all things, and turn your *desire* away from them; do not *desire* them, nor seek to *possess* that for *your own* which is *something*. For as soon as you take *something* into your *desire*, and receive it into yourself for *your own*, then that *something* is the same as yourself, and it works with you in your *will*; then you are obliged to protect it and to take care of it as your own being. But if you receive nothing into your *desire*, then you are free from all things and rule over all things at once. For you have received nothing for *your own*, and are *nothing* to all things, and all things are *nothing* to you. You are as a child who does not understand what a thing is; or though you do understand it, yet you understand it without being *affected* by it, in the same manner that GOD rules and sees all things, and yet nothing comprehends or captivates him.

You also desired that I would teach you how you might attain it. Consider then the *words of Christ*, who said, ‘Without me you can do nothing.’ In your *own power* you cannot come to such *rest* that no *creature* should touch you, unless you give yourself wholly up to the *life* of our *Lord Jesus Christ*, and *resign* your *will* and *desire* wholly to Him, and *will* nothing without Him. Then with your *body* you are in the world, in the *properties*, and with your *reason* under the *cross* of our *Lord Christ*; but with your *will* you walk in *heaven*, and are in the *original* from which all *creatures* proceeded, and to which they go again. And then you may behold all things outwardly with your *reason*, and inwardly with your *mind*, and rule in and over all things with *Christ*, to whom *all power is given both in heaven and on earth*.

10. THE SCHOLAR.—O Master, the *creatures* that live in me so withhold me that I cannot *wholly* yield and give up myself as I willingly would.

THE MASTER.—If your *will* goes forth from the *creatures*, then the *creatures* are forsaken in and by you; they are in the *world*, and your *body* only is with the *creatures*, but in your *spirit* you walk with GOD. And if your *will* leaves the *creatures*, then the *creatures* are dead in it, and live only in the *body* in the *world*; and if your *will* does not bring itself into them, they cannot touch your soul. For *St. Paul* says, ‘Our *citizenship is in heaven*’; also, ‘You are the temple of the *Holy Spirit*, who dwells in you’; so then, the *Holy Spirit* dwells in the *will*, and the *creatures* dwell in the *body*.

11. THE SCHOLAR.—If the *Holy Spirit* dwells in the *will* of

the *mind*, how may I keep myself so that He may not depart from me?
**THE MASTER**.—Observe the *words* of our *Lord Jesus Christ*: ‘If you abide in my *word*, then my *words* abide in you.’ If you abide with your *will* in the *words of Christ*, then His *word* and *spirit* abide in you. But if your *will* goes into the *creatures*, then you have broken yourself off from Him, and then you cannot keep yourself in any other way but by abiding *continually in resigned humility* and entering into *continual repentance*, so that you are *always* grieved that *creatures* live in you. If you do this, you stand in a *daily dying* from the *creatures*, and in a *daily ascending* into heaven in and with your *will* again.
12. **THE SCHOLAR**.—O loving Master, please teach me *how* I may come into such *continual repentance*.

---

### *How Men may come to continual Repentance, and subsist in Temptation.*

**THE MASTER**.—When you *leave* that which *loves* you, and love that which *hates* you, then you may abide *continually in repentance*.
13. **THE SCHOLAR**.—What is it that I must *leave* in this way?
**THE MASTER**.—Your *creatures* in flesh and blood, and all other things that *love* you; they love you because your *will* entertains, nourishes, and preserves them. These the *will* must *leave* and account as enemies; and the *cross* of our *Lord Jesus Christ*, together with the *reproach* of the world, *hates* you. You must learn to *love* these, and take them as a daily exercise for your *repentance*. And then you shall have continual cause to *hate yourself* in the creature, and to seek the *eternal rest* in which your *will* may *rest*; as *Christ* said,
> ‘In the world you shall have
> tribulation, but in me you shall have rest.’
14. **THE SCHOLAR**.—*How* may I stand firm in a temptation such as this?
**THE MASTER**.—If every hour you once throw yourself beyond all *creatures*, above all *sensual reason*, into the *mere mercy of* GOD, into the *sufferings* of our *Lord*, and *yield* yourself into them, then you shall receive *power* to rule over *sin, death*, and the *devil*, over *hell* and the world, and then you may stand firm in all temptations.
15. **THE SCHOLAR**.—Poor man that I am, what would become of me if I should reach with my *mind* to *that* place where no creature is!

<summary>The Master explains that maintaining a connection with God requires abiding in Christ's word and practicing a daily "dying" to worldly attachments and self-will. He teaches that by embracing the cross and yielding to God's mercy, a person gains the spiritual power to overcome temptation and the influence of the world.</summary>
<keywords>repentance, word of Christ, creatures, humility, temptation, cross of Christ, resignation, self-denial, spiritual power</keywords>

--- Page 122 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue on the super-sensual life, where the Master explains how the soul can transcend the created world to experience God's glory and the resulting conflict with the worldly life.</meta>

<page-num>99</page-num>
<header>Of the Super-Sensual Life.</header>

---

THE MASTER.—Loving Scholar, if your *Will* could break itself away for *one hour* from all *creatures*, and throw itself into *that* where no *creature* is, it would immediately be clothed with the *highest splendor of* GOD'S *glory*, and would taste in itself the sweetest *love* of our *Lord Jesus*, which no man can express; and it would find in itself the unspeakable *words* of our *Lord* concerning His *great mercy*. It would feel in itself that the *cross* of our *Lord Christ* would be very pleasing to it, and it would love *that* more than the honor and goods of the *world*.

16. THE SCHOLAR.—But what would become of the *body*, since it must live among *creatures*?

THE MASTER.—The *body* would be placed into the imitation of our LORD *Christ*, who said *His kingdom* was not of this world. It would begin to die from *without* and from *within*; <note>Ink smudge over the start of this line</note> from *without*, it would die to the *vanity* and evil *deeds* of the *world*; it would be an utter enemy to all *pride* and *insolence*. And from *within*, it would die to all *evil lusts* and *envy*, and would gain a *mind* and *will* within itself that is wholly *new*, which would be continually directed toward GOD and goodness.

17. THE SCHOLAR.—But the *world* would *hate* and *despise* a man for doing so, seeing as he must contradict the *world*, and live and act differently than the *world does*.

THE MASTER.—He would not take that as any harm done to him, but would rather rejoice that he has become worthy to be thus conformed to the example of our *Lord Christ*, and be willing to bear *that cross* following our *Lord*, simply so that the Lord might bestow the influence of His sweet *love* upon him.

18. THE SCHOLAR.—What would become of him when the *anger of God from within*, and the *wicked world from without*, should assault him, as happened to our *Lord Christ*?

THE MASTER.—Let him respond to that just as our *Lord Christ did*. When He was reproached, reviled, and crucified by the *world* and the *priests*, He commended His soul into the hands of His Father, and so departed from the *anguish of this world* into *eternal joy*. In the same way, such a soul would escape the *reproach* and *anguish* of the whole *world*, and penetrate into itself, into the great *love* of God, and be sustained and refreshed by the most sweet name *Jesus*, and see and find within itself a *new world* springing forth through the *anger of God*; then it should wrap itself up in that, and consider all things equal. And thus, whether the *body* is in *hell* or on *earth*, yet the soul would be in the *greatest love of* GOD.

19. THE SCHOLAR.—But how would a man support himself in the *world*, and those who belong to him, if he should thus incur the displeasure of the whole *world*?

THE MASTER.—He thereby gains greater favor than the *world* is able to bestow, for he has GOD and *all His angels* for his *friends*, who protect him in all dangers and necessities. GOD is his *blessing* in everything, and though sometimes it may seem as if GOD would not *bless* him, it is only to *test* him, and is in truth the *drawing* of GOD'S *love*, so that he might the *more earnestly* pray to GOD and commit all his ways to Him.

20. THE SCHOLAR.—But he *loses* all his *good friends*, and there will be no one to help him in his necessity.

THE MASTER.—No, he *gains* the hearts of all his *good friends* into his possession, and *loses* none but his *enemies*, who previously loved his *vanity* and *wickedness*.

21. THE SCHOLAR.—*How* is it that he *gains* his *good friends* into his possession?

THE MASTER.—He *gains* the souls of all those who belong to our *Lord Jesus* to be his *brothers* and the *members* of his own *life*. For the children of GOD are but *one* in *Christ*, which *one is Christ in all*. Therefore he gains them all to be his *fellow-members* in the *body of Christ*, for they have the *heavenly goods* in common and live in one and the same *love* of GOD, just as the branches of a tree spring from one and the same *sap*. Nor will he lack outward natural friends any more than our *Lord Christ* did. For though the high priests and rulers of the *world* who did not belong to Him, nor were His members and brothers, did not love Him, yet those who were capable of receiving His *words* loved *Him*. So likewise those who love *truth* and *righteousness* would love that man and associate themselves with him; as *Nicodemus* did with *Christ*, who came to Him by night and *in his heart* loved *Jesus* for the *truth's sake*, yet outwardly feared the *world*. And thus shall many *friends* rise up for such a man who were not known to him at the beginning.

22. THE SCHOLAR.—But it is very painful to be *despised* by all the *world*.

THE MASTER.—That which *now* seems hard and heavy to you, you will *afterward* love most of all.

23. THE SCHOLAR.—How can it be that I should *love* that which *hates* me?

THE MASTER.—Though you *love* the earthly *wisdom* now, yet when you are clothed with the *heavenly*, you will see that all the *wisdom* of the *world* is but *folly*, and that the *world hates* only your *enemy, namely*, the *mortal life*; and when you yourself come to hate the *will* of it, then you also will begin to *love* that despising of the *mortal life*.

24. THE SCHOLAR.—But *how* can these two stand together, *namely*, that a man should both *love* and *hate* himself?

<summary>The Master explains that the spiritual seeker gains the fellowship of all believers in the body of Christ and the protection of angels, even if the worldly despise them. He teaches that as one gains heavenly wisdom, the rejection by the world is seen as a rejection of the mortal self, which the soul must learn to despise.</summary>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Master and the Scholar regarding the "super-sensual life," specifically exploring the relationship between divine love and the individual self, and why love must coexist with suffering.</meta>

<page-num>101</page-num>
<header>Of the Super-Sensual Life. 101</header>

THE MASTER.—In loving yourself this way, you do not love *yourself* as your *own*, but as something given from the *Love of GOD*. You love the *Divine Ground* within you, through which you love the *Divine Wisdom*, GOD’S works of wonder, and *your brothers*. But in *hating* yourself, you *hate* only that which is *your own*, where *evil* clings closely to you; and you do this so that you may completely destroy within you *that* which you call *yours*, when you say "*I*" or "*myself*" do this or that. *This* you would completely destroy in yourself, so that you may become a *ground* that is entirely *divine*. Love hates *self*, or that which we call *I*, because it is a deadly thing, and the two—namely, *Love* and *Self*—cannot easily stand together. For *Love* possesses *Heaven* and dwells in *itself*; but that which I call *I* possesses the *world* and *worldly things*, and also dwells in *itself*. And as *Heaven* rules the world, and as *eternity* rules *time*, so *Love* rules over the *natural life*.

25. THE SCHOLAR.—Loving Master, please tell me *why* *Love* and *trouble*, friend and foe, must be together; would not *Love* alone be better?

---

->### *How Love and sorrow exist together as one; and what Love is, what its power, virtue, height, and greatness are; and where it dwells in man; also the nearest way to attain it.*<-

THE MASTER.—If *Love* did not dwell in *trouble*, it would have nothing to *love*; but since its *substance* which it loves—namely, the poor soul—is in trouble and pain, *Love* has cause to give up its own *substance* in order to deliver its *beloved* from pain, so that it might also be loved in return. Indeed, it could not be known what *Love* is if it did not have *something* to *love*.

26. THE SCHOLAR.—What is the *virtue*, *power*, *height*, and *greatness* of love?

THE MASTER.—Its *virtue* is that *nothing* from which all things proceed, and its *power* is in and through all things; its *height* is as high as GOD, and its *greatness* is greater than GOD. <note>Here is meant the manifestation of the Deity in the glorious eternal light of the Divine Essence.</note> Whoever finds it, finds *nothing* and all things.

27. THE SCHOLAR.—Loving Master, please tell me how I may understand this.

THE MASTER.—What I meant by saying, "*its virtue is that nothing from which all things proceed*," you may understand in this way.

<summary>The Master explains that divine love requires the denial of the selfish "I" and must exist alongside suffering so that it has an object to redeem and love. He describes the mystical nature of Love as a power that is both "nothing" and "everything," surpassing even the manifestation of God.</summary>
<keywords>self-denial, divine love, suffering, the soul, Divine Ground, nothingness, manifestation of God, mystical theology</keywords>

--- Page 125 ---
<meta>This page continues the Master’s explanation of the nature of Love, elaborating on the paradoxical descriptions given previously—that Love is higher than God, greater than God, and is both "nothing" and "all things."</meta>

<page-num>102</page-num>
<header>The Way to Christ.</header>

When you have completely gone out from the *creature*, and become *nothing* to all that is *Nature* and *creature*, then you are in that *eternal One* who is GOD Himself, and then you shall perceive and feel the highest *virtue* of *Love*.

But as to what I said, that "*its power is in and through all things*," you shall perceive and find that in your own *soul* and *body*. When this great *Love* is kindled in you, it will *burn* more than any *fire* can do. You shall also see in all the works of GOD that *Love* has poured itself into all things, and is the most *inward* and most *outward ground* in all things. *Inwardly*, in the *virtue* and *power*; and *outwardly*, in the *figure*, *form*, and *shape* of everything.

The meaning of what I said—namely, "*its height is as high as God*"—you may also come to understand in yourself, because it will bring you to be as *high* as GOD himself *is* <image-desc>manicule</image-desc> as *may* be seen in our beloved *Lord Christ* in our humanity; which humanity *Love* has brought into the *highest throne*, even into the *power of the Deity*.

But that which I further said, "*its greatness is greater than God*," is also true. For *Love* enters into that where GOD does *not* dwell, as when our beloved *Lord Christ* was in *Hell*. *Hell* was *not* GOD, but *Love* was there and destroyed *Death*. Also, when you are in *anguish* and *trouble* of *soul*, GOD is not the *anguish* or *trouble*, but His *Love* is there, and brings you out of *anguish* into GOD. When GOD *hides* Himself in you, *Love* is there and makes Him manifest in you.

And that which I said, "*whoever finds it, finds nothing and all things*," is also true; for he finds a *supernatural, supersensual abyss* having no *ground*, where there is no place to dwell. He finds also nothing that is like it, and therefore it may be compared to nothing; for it is deeper than anything, and is as *nothing* to all things, for it is not comprehensible. And because it is *nothing*, it is free from all things, and is that only *Good* which a man cannot describe or express *what it is*.

But that which I said last, "*he who finds it, finds all things*," is also true; it has been the *beginning* of all things, and it rules all things. If you find it, you come into that *ground* from which all things proceeded, and in which they subsist; and you are in it a *king* over all the works of GOD.

28. THE SCHOLAR.—Loving Master, please tell me, *where* does it dwell in man?

THE MASTER.—Where man does *not* dwell, there it has its seat in man.

29. THE SCHOLAR.—*Where* is that in a man's self where man does *not* dwell?

<summary>The Master explains that divine Love is the foundational power of all existence, paradoxically described as both "nothing" and "all things," which can bring the human soul into the very power of the Deity. He concludes by stating that Love dwells within man precisely where the self-centered "man" is absent.</summary>
<keywords>divine love, nature, creature, Lord Christ, Deity, Hell, death, anguish, supersensual abyss, the ground of being, self-denial</keywords>

--- Page 126 ---
<meta>This page continues the Master’s explanation of Love as "nothing" and "all things," moving into the practical application of how the soul must die to its own will to allow Love to dwell within it.</meta>

<page-num>103</page-num>
-># Of the Super-Sensual Life.<-

THE MASTER.—It is in the soul that is *resigned* to the Ground, where the soul dies to *its own will*, and *wills* no more of *itself*, but only what GOD wills. That is the place in a man's self where man dwells *not*, and there *Love* dwells. For as much of the soul as *its own will* is dead to *itself in*, so much place *Love* has taken up within it; for where *its own will* sat before, there now is *nothing*, and where *nothing* is, there the *Love* of GOD is working alone.

30. THE SCHOLAR.—But *how* may I comprehend or attain it without the *dying* of my *will*?

THE MASTER.—If you will comprehend it *in your own will*, it flies away from you; but if you *yield* yourself wholly up to it, then you are dead to yourself *in your own will*, and *Love* will then be the *Life of your nature*. It does not kill you, but *quickens* you according to its *Life*, and then you *live*, yet not to *your own will*, but to *its will*; for *your will* becomes *its will*, and then you are dead to yourself, but *alive unto God*.

31. THE SCHOLAR.—How is it that so few find it, when all would so gladly have it?

THE MASTER.—Because they all seek it in *something*, *namely*, in an *imaginary opinion*, in *self-desire*, for which almost all have a peculiar natural lust or inclination. And though *Love* should proffer itself to them, it could find no harbor or dwelling-place in them, because the *imagination* that is in *their own will* has set itself in the place of it. And that *imagination* of *self-lust* would gladly have the *Love* in it; but *Love* flies away, for it dwells only in *nothing*, and therefore such souls do not find it.

32. THE SCHOLAR.—What is the office of it in *nothing*?

THE MASTER.—Its office is to *penetrate* without intermission into *something*; and if it finds a place in *something* which *stands still*, then its office is to take possession of it, and to *rejoice* in it with its *flaming fire of Love*, more than the sun in this world; and without intermission to kindle a *fire* in *something*, and to consume the *something*, and to inflame itself with it.

33. THE SCHOLAR.—O loving Master! How shall I understand this?

THE MASTER.—If it may but kindle a *fire* in you, you shall *feel* how it consumes your *selfhood*, that which you call *I*, and *rejoices* so exceedingly in your *fire*, that you would rather suffer yourself to be *killed*, than to enter again into your *something*. Its *flame* also is so great, that it would not leave you, though it should cost your temporal life; it would go with you in its *fire into death*; indeed, if you went into *Hell*, it would even destroy *Hell* for your sake.

34. THE SCHOLAR.—Loving Master, I can no longer endure

<summary>The Master explains that Love can only dwell in a soul that has surrendered its own self-will and become "nothing." He describes how Love's fire consumes the ego (selfhood) to replace it with a divine life that can overcome even death and Hell.</summary>
<keywords>resignation, self-will, Ground of the soul, Love of God, selfhood, nothingness, spiritual death, quickening</keywords>

...that anything should divert me from this *Love*; *how shall* I find the *nearest Way* to it?

THE MASTER.—Where the Way is *hardest*, walk there, and take up what the *World rejects*; and what the *World* does, do not do. Walk contrary to the *World* in all things, and then you take the *nearest Way* to it.

35. THE SCHOLAR.—If I should walk contrary to the *World* in everything, I must necessarily live in mere *Misery* and *Restlessness*; and I should also be considered a *Fool*.

THE MASTER.—I do not command you to do harm to anyone; but because the *World* loves only *Deceit* and *Vanity*, and walks in false and wicked ways; therefore, if you will act a completely contrary part to its ways in all things, walk only in the *Right Way*. For the *Right Way* is contrary to all the *Ways* of the *World*.

But since you say *you would live in mere Anguish and Trouble*: that indeed will be so *according to the Flesh*; and will give you occasion for *continual Repentance*; and in such *Anxiety* the *Love* will most readily kindle its *Fire*.

What you also say, *namely*, '*that you should be considered a fool*,' is true; for the Way to the *Love* of GOD is *Folly* to the *World*, but *Wisdom* to the *Children of God*. When the *World* perceives this *Fire of Love* kindled in the *Children of God*, it says they have become *Fools*; but to the *Children of God* it is the greatest Treasure; so great that no life can express, nor tongue describe, what the *Fire of the inflaming Love of* GOD is. It is *brighter* than the Sun, and *sweeter* than anything. It is far more *nourishing* and *vital* than any food or drink, and more *pleasant* than all the joy of this world. Whoever gains it is *richer* than any king on Earth, more noble than any emperor, and more *potent* and strong than all authority and power.

---

->### *Where the blessed and damned Souls go, when they depart from their Bodies; and how Heaven and Hell are in Man.*<-

36. Then the Scholar asked his Master further, saying, *Where* do the souls, both of the *Saved* and *Damned*, go when they leave these mortal bodies?

His Master answered, The soul needs no *going forth*; only the *outward mortal life* and the *earthly body* at *Death* separate themselves from the soul. The soul has *Heaven* and *Hell* in itself already; as it is written, 'The Kingdom of God comes...

<summary>The Master explains that the path to Divine Love requires walking contrary to the world's vanity, which the world views as folly but God's children recognize as the greatest treasure. He also teaches that Heaven and Hell are internal states of the soul rather than external destinations reached after death.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, The Way to Christ, Fire of Love, repentance, worldliness, spiritual wisdom, soul's destination, internal heaven, internal hell</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the Master's discourse on the nature of the Super-Sensual life, shifting from the practice of walking contrary to the world to the internal reality of the Kingdom of God and the states of Heaven and Hell.</meta>

'not with outward observation; neither shall they say, look here,
'or look there it is; for behold, the Kingdom of God is within you.'
And whichever of the two states, *namely*, either *Heaven* or *Hell*, shall
be manifested in the soul, in *that* state it remains.

37. THE SCHOLAR.—Does it not *enter* into *Heaven* or *Hell*,
as a man *enters* into a house, or *goes* through a door or pas-
sage, into another *place*?

THE MASTER.—No, there is no such kind of *entering*; for
*Heaven* and *Hell* are present *everywhere*, and it is only the *turning*
of the *will* either into God's *love*, or into His *anger*, that intro-
duces one into them. And *that* comes to pass in *this life*, accord-
ing to the saying of St. *Paul*, 'Our conversation is in Heaven;'
and *Christ* also says, 'My sheep hear my voice, and I know
'them, and they follow me, and I give to them eternal life,
'and none shall pluck them out of my hand.'

38. THE SCHOLAR.—How does this *entering* of the *will*
into *Heaven* or *Hell* happen?

THE MASTER.—When the *ground* of the *will* yields itself
up to GOD, then it sinks down from *itself* beyond all *ground*
and *place*, where GOD alone is manifest, works, and wills; and
then it becomes *nothing* to itself, regarding *its own willing*, and so
GOD works and wills in it. Yes, GOD *dwells* in this *re-*
*signed will*; by which means the soul is sanctified, and so
comes into *Divine Rest*. Now when the body is dead, the soul
is thoroughly penetrated and saturated with the *love* of GOD;
and thoroughly enlightened with the *light* of GOD; even as fire
thoroughly inflames a red-hot iron, by which it loses its dark-
ness. This is the *Right Hand of Christ*; where the *love* of GOD
thoroughly possesses the soul all over, and is a *shining light*
and a *new life* in it; and thus the soul is in *Heaven*, and a
*temple of the Holy Spirit*; yes, it is indeed *itself* the very *Heaven*
of GOD in which He dwells. But the case of the *wicked* soul is
*this*: It will not, during the time of this life, enter into the *divine*
*resignation* of its *will*, but goes on continually in *its own lust*
and *falsehood*, in the *will of the devil*. It receives into itself
nothing but *wickedness*, *lies*, *pride*, *covetousness*, *envy*, and
*anger*, and yields its *will* to them. By which means the
*hellish element* also becomes manifest and active in the
soul, and thoroughly penetrates and possesses it all over, as
fire does a burning hot iron. Now such a soul cannot come to
*Divine Rest*, for GOD's *anger* is manifested in it. And therefore
when the body departs from it, then its *eternal grief* and *despair*
begin. For it perceives and finds that it has become a mere
*self-tormenting abomination*, and is ashamed to strive to enter
with its *false will* into GOD. No, it cannot; for it is captivated

<summary>The Master explains that Heaven and Hell are not physical locations but internal spiritual states determined by whether the human will yields to God's love or his anger. A soul that resigns its will to God becomes a temple of the Holy Spirit, while one that persists in its own lusts becomes a self-tormented abomination.</summary>
<keywords>Kingdom of God, Heaven, Hell, the Will, Divine Resignation, Divine Rest, Holy Spirit, self-torment, spiritual transformation</keywords>

<header>The Way to Christ.</header>

in the *Wrath*, and is itself *mere Wrath*, and has shut itself up therein by its *false* and *evil Desire*, which it has raised up in itself. And seeing the *Light of GOD* shines not in it, and his *Love* touches it not, therefore it is a *great Darkness* and an *aching, anguishing Source of Fire*, which carries *Hell* in itself, and cannot reach the *Light of GOD*. Thus it dwells in *itself* in *Hell*, and needs no *entering* into it, for wherever it is, it is in *Hell*; though it should cast itself many hundred thousand miles from the place it is in, yet it still abides in the same *property* and *source of darkness*.

39. THE SCHOLAR.—How does it come to pass, then, that a *Holy Soul* in this lifetime does not perfectly perceive that *Heavenly Light* and *Joy*, nor a *wicked Soul* feel *Hell*, when both of them are thus in man, and *one of them of necessity works in him*?

THE MASTER.—The *Kingdom of Heaven* in the saints is *working* and perceptible in their *Faith*; they feel the *Love of GOD* in their *Faith*, by which the *Will* yields itself up to GOD. But the *natural life* is surrounded with *flesh* and *blood*, stands in the contrariety of *GOD’s anger*, and is also encompassed and closely beset with the *vain lust of this outward world*. So that the poor soul lives in the midst of its *enemies*, having the *world on one side*, the *devil on the other*, and on a *third* the *curse of the anger of GOD in flesh and blood*; which continually *sift, try*, and thoroughly *penetrate* the life of man, who is often brought into bitter *anguish* through these several *assaults* of *Hell*, which would gladly manifest itself in him. But the soul sinks down in the *hope of divine grace*, and stands like a fair rose in the midst of thorns, until the kingdom of *this world* falls away from it in the *death of the body*; and then does it *first* truly and really become manifest in the *love of GOD*, having nothing more to hinder or molest it. But during the time of this life in the body, it must *walk with Christ*, who will stand by it and deliver it out of *its own hell* by thoroughly *penetrating* it with his *love*, and thus change its *hell* into *heaven*.

But to your question, *why do the wicked not feel hell in the time of this life?* I answer: he *has* it indeed, and sometimes *feels* it in his *evil conscience*, but not perfectly, nor does he *know* that it is *hell*. For he still possesses earthly *vanity*, with which he is enamored, and in which he takes *delight* and *pleasure*; also the *outward life* still has the *light* of *outward nature*, and so the *hellish pain* cannot be *fully* manifested and felt. But when the *body dies*, and the soul can no longer enjoy *temporal pleasure*, or be cheered with the *light of this outward world*, then it stands in *eternal hunger* and *thirst* after such *vanity* as it was in love with here. Yet it can reach

<summary>The Master explains that Heaven and Hell are internal states that the soul carries within itself, though they are partially obscured by the physical body and the distractions of the material world. Upon death, the soul’s chosen state—either the love of God or the hunger of hellish vanity—becomes fully manifest.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the Master’s discourse on the nature of the spiritual versus the visible world, explaining that the transition between Heaven and Hell occurs within the soul upon the death of the physical body.</meta>

<page-num>108</page-num>
<header>The Way to Christ.</header>

Hell also exists throughout the whole World, and dwells and works only within itself, and in that where the *Foundation of Hell* is manifested—namely, in the *Self*, and in the false and *evil Will*. The *visible World* has both *Heaven* and *Hell* in it. Man, regarding his temporal *Life*, belongs only to the *visible World*, and therefore during the time of this *Life* he does not see the *spiritual World*. For the *outward World* with its *Substance* is a *Cover* to the *spiritual World*, just as the *Body* is to the *Soul*. But when the *outward Man* dies, then the *spiritual World* is manifested in and to the Soul, either in the *eternal Light* with the *holy Angels*, or in the *eternal Darkness* with the *Devils*.

43. THE SCHOLAR.— *What is an Angel or the Soul of a Man, that they may be manifested thus, either in GOD’S Love or Anger?*

THE MASTER.— They came from one *Origin*; they are *Branches* or *Out-births* of the *Divine Science* of the *Divine Will*, sprung from the *Divine Word*, and made Objects of the *Divine Love*. They have come out of the *Ground of Eternity*, from which *Light* and *Darkness* spring—namely, *Darkness* consisting in harboring and cherishing *Self-desire*, and *Light* consisting in *Willing* the same as GOD. In this *Willing*, the *Love* of GOD readily and gladly works; but in receiving and entertaining the *Self* in the *Willing* of the Soul, GOD’S *Will* works in *Pain*, and becomes a *Darkness* so that the *Light* may be known. Thus *Heaven* and *Hell* are nothing else but a *Manifestation* of the *Divine Will*, either in *Light* or *Darkness*, according to the *Properties* of the *spiritual World*.

44. THE SCHOLAR.— *What then is the Body of Man?*

---

### *What the Body of Man is; and why the Soul is capable of receiving Good and Evil.*

THE MASTER.— It is the *visible World*; an *Image* and *Quintessence* or *Compound* of all that the *World* is; and the *visible World* is a *Manifestation* of the *inward spiritual World*, having come out of the *eternal Light* and out of the *eternal Darkness*, out of the *spiritual Compaction* or *Connection*; and it is also an *Image* or *Figure* of *Eternity*, by which *Eternity* has made itself *visible*; where *Self Will* and *resigned Will*—namely, *Evil* and *Good*—work with one another.

Such a *Substance* is the *outward Man*. For GOD created *Man* out of the *outward World*, and breathed into him the *inward spiritual World* for a *Soul* and an *intelligent Life*, and therefore in the *Things* of the *outward World*, *Man* can receive and work both *evil* and *good*.

<summary>The Master explains that the visible world masks the spiritual world of Heaven and Hell, and that the human soul originates from the Divine Will, possessing the capacity for both light and darkness based on its alignment with God or self-desire.</summary>
<keywords>Hell, Heaven, visible world, spiritual world, Divine Will, Divine Word, soul, outward man, self-desire, quintessence</keywords>

45. THE SCHOLAR.—*What shall happen after this world, when all things perish and come to an end?*

---

### *Of the Destruction of the World; of Man's Body in and after the Resurrection; where Heaven and Hell shall be; of the Last Judgment; and why there must be Strife in the Creature.*

THE MASTER.—The material *substance* only ceases; namely, the four elements, the sun, moon, and stars. And then the *inward world* will be entirely visible and manifest. But whatever has been created by the will or *spirit* of man during this *world’s time*, whether *evil* or *good*—I say, *every such work* shall then separate itself in a *spiritual manner*, into either the *eternal light* or the *eternal darkness*. For that which is born from each *will* penetrates and passes again into that which is *like itself*. And there, the darkness is called *hell*, and is an eternal forgetting of *all good*; and the light is called the *kingdom of God*, and is an eternal *joy* in and for the saints, who continually glorify and praise GOD for having delivered them from the torment of evil.

The *Last Judgment* is a kindling of the *fire* of both GOD’S *love* and *anger*, in which the *matter* of every *substance* perishes, and each *fire* shall attract into itself *its own*—that is, the *substance* that is *like itself*. Thus GOD’S *fire* of *love* will draw into it whatever is born in the *love* of GOD, or the *love-principle*, in which it shall also burn in the manner of *love*, and yield itself into that *substance*. But the *torment* will draw into itself what is created in the *anger* of GOD in *darkness*, and consume the *false substance*; and then there will remain only the *painful, aching will* in its own proper *nature, image, and figure*.

46. THE SCHOLAR.—With what *matter* and *form* shall the human *body* rise?

THE MASTER.—*It is sown a natural, gross, and elementary body*, which in this lifetime is like the outward elements; yet in this gross *body* there is a subtle *power and virtue*. Just as in the *earth* there is also a subtle *good virtue* which is like the sun and is one and the same with the sun; which also in the beginning of *time* sprang and proceeded out of the *divine power and virtue*, from which all the *good virtue of the body* is likewise derived. This good *virtue* of the mortal *body* shall return and live forever in a kind of *transparent crystalline* material property, in *spiritual flesh and blood*; as shall also the

<summary>The Master explains that when the material world ends, only its physical elements perish, while the spiritual results of human deeds are sorted into eternal light or darkness. He describes the Last Judgment as a separation by the fires of God's love and anger and states that the resurrected body will be a spiritualized, crystalline form of its original inner power.</summary>
<keywords>Last Judgment, resurrection, spiritual body, eternity, divine love, divine anger, material substance, elements, heaven, hell</keywords>

good *virtue* of the *earth*, for the *earth* likewise shall become *crystalline*, and the *divine light* shine in every thing that has a *being*, *essence*, or *substance*. And as the *gross earth* shall perish and never return, so also the *gross flesh of man* shall perish and not live forever. But *all things* must *appear* before the *judgment*, and in the *judgment* be separated by the *fire*; yes, both the *earth* and also the *ashes* of the *human body*. For when GOD shall once move the *spiritual world*, *every spirit* shall attract its *spiritual substance* to itself. A *good spirit* and *soul* shall draw to itself its *good substance*, and an *evil one* its *evil substance*. But we must here understand by *substance* such a *material power* and *virtue*, the *essence* of which is *mere virtue*, like a *material tincture* (such a thing as has all *figures*, *colors* and *virtues* in it, and is at the same time *transparent*), the *grossness* of which has *perished* in *all things*.

47. THE SCHOLAR.—Shall we not rise again with our *visible bodies*, and live in them forever? *See the Forty Questions of the Soul, 21st Question, 12th Verse.*

THE MASTER.—When the *visible world* perishes, then *all* that which has come out of it, and has been *external*, shall perish with it. There shall remain of the *world* only the *heavenly crystalline nature* and *form*, and of *man* also only the *spiritual earth*; for *man* shall be then wholly like the *spiritual world*, which as yet is hidden.

48. THE SCHOLAR.—Shall there be *husband* and *wife*, or *children* or *kindred*, in the *heavenly life*, or shall one associate with another, as they do in this *life*?

THE MASTER.—Why are you so *fleshly-minded*? There will be neither *husband* nor *wife*, but all will be like the *angels of God*, namely, masculine virgins. There will be neither *son* nor *daughter*, *brother* nor *sister*, but all of one *stock* and *kindred*. For all are but *one* in *Christ*, as a *tree* and its *branches* are *one*, though distinct as *creatures*; but GOD is *all in all*. Indeed, there will be *spiritual knowledge* of what everyone has *been* and done, but no *possessing* or *enjoying*, or *desire* of possessing earthly *things*, or *enjoying* fleshly *relations* anymore.

49. THE SCHOLAR.—Shall they *all* have that *eternal joy* and *glorification* alike?

THE MASTER.—The Scripture says, "Such as the people are, such is their God." And in another place, "With the holy you are *holy*, and with the perverse you are *perverse*." And St. Paul says, "In the resurrection one shall differ from another in *glory*, as do the *sun, moon, and stars*." Therefore know, that

<summary>The Master explains that the physical world and body will be transformed into a crystalline spiritual substance, where earthly relationships and gender cease to exist. In the resurrection, individuals will share a unified existence in Christ, yet their degree of heavenly glory will vary according to their spiritual nature.</summary>

The Blessed shall indeed *all* enjoy the *Divine Working* in and upon them; but their *Virtue*, and *Illumination*, or *Glory*, shall be very different, according to how they have been endowed in this Life with different Measures and Degrees of *Power* and *Virtue* in their *painful Working*. For the *painful Working* of the *Creature* in this lifetime is the opening and begetting of *Divine Power*, by which that Power is made *movable* and *operative*. Now those who have worked with *Christ* in this lifetime, and not in the *Lust of the Flesh*, shall have *great Power* and *transcendent Glorification* in and upon them. But others, who have only expected and relied upon an *imputed Satisfaction*, and in the meantime have served their *Belly-God* <note>One who makes a god of their own appetite or physical desires; a glutton.</note>, and yet at last have turned and obtained *Grace*; those, I say, shall not attain to so high a Degree of *Power* and *Illumination*. So that there will be as great a *Difference of Degrees* between them as there is between the Sun, Moon, and Stars; or between the Flowers of the Field in their Varieties of *Beauty*, *Power*, and *Virtue*.

50. THE SCHOLAR.—*How shall the World be judged, and by whom?*

THE MASTER.—*Jesus Christ*, that *Word of God* which became Man, shall by the Power of His *Divine Stirring* or *Motion* separate from Himself all that does not belong to Him, and shall wholly manifest *His Kingdom* in the *Place* or *Space* where *this World* now is; for the separating *Motion* works all over the Universe, through everything at once.

51. THE SCHOLAR.—*Where shall the Devils and all the damned be thrown, when the Place of this World is the Kingdom of Christ, and as such shall be glorified? Shall they be cast out of the Place of this World? or shall Christ have and manifest His Dominion out of the Sphere or Place of this World?*

THE MASTER.—Hell shall remain in the *Place* or *Sphere* of this World everywhere, but hidden from the Kingdom of Heaven, as the Night is hidden in and to the Day. ‘The Light shall shine forever in the Darkness, but the Darkness can never comprehend, or reach, it.’ And the *Light* is the *Kingdom of Christ*; but the *Darkness* is *Hell*, in which the *Devils* and the *Wicked* dwell; and thus *they* shall be suppressed by the *Kingdom of Christ*, and made His *Footstool*, namely, a *Reproach*.

52. THE SCHOLAR.—*How shall all People and Nations be brought to Judgment?*

THE MASTER.—The *Eternal Word of* GOD, out of which every *Spiritual Creaturely Life* has proceeded, will *move itself* at that Hour, according to *Love* and *Anger*, in every *Life* which has come out of *Eternity*, and will draw every *Creature* before the *Judgment of Christ*, to be sentenced by this *Motion* of the *Word*.

<summary>The Master explains that while all the blessed enjoy God, their degrees of glory differ based on their earthly spiritual labors. He further describes the Last Judgment as a separation where Hell remains a hidden, suppressed darkness within the same space as Christ's glorified Kingdom.</summary>

The *Life* will then be manifested in all its *Works*, and every *Soul* shall see and feel its *Judgment* and *Sentence* within itself. For the *Judgment* is, indeed, immediately manifested in and to every *Soul* at the *Departure* of the *Body*: And the *Last Judgment* is but a *Return* of the *Spiritual Body*, and a *Separation* of the *World*, when the *Evil* shall be separated from the *Good*, in the *Substance* of the *World*, and of the human *Body*, and every *Thing* shall enter into its *eternal Receptacle*. And thus is it a *Manifestation* of the *Mystery* of *GOD* in every *Substance* and *Life*.

53. THE SCHOLAR. *How will the Sentence be pronounced?*
THE MASTER. Here consider the *Words of Christ*. ‘He will
‘say to those on his Right Hand, Come, you blessed of my
‘Father, inherit the Kingdom prepared for you from the Founda-
‘tion of the World. For I was hungry and you gave me Food, I
‘was thirsty and you gave me Drink; I was a Stranger and you
‘took me in; naked and you clothed me. I was sick, and you
‘visited me, in Prison, and you came to me.
‘Then shall they answer him, saying LORD, when did we see you
‘hungry, thirsty, a Stranger, naked, sick, or in Prison, and
‘ministered thus to you?
‘Then shall the King answer and say to them; inasmuch
‘as you have done it to one of the least of these my Brothers,
‘you have done it to me.
‘And to the wicked on his Left Hand he will say, depart
‘from me, you cursed, into everlasting Fire, prepared for the
‘Devil and his Angels. For I was hungry, thirsty, a Stranger,
‘naked, sick, and in Prison, and you did not minister to me.
‘And they shall also answer him and say, When did we see
‘you thus, and did not minister to you?
‘And he will answer them, Verily I say to you, inasmuch
‘as you have not done it to one of the least of these, you did it
‘not to me.
‘And these shall depart into everlasting Punishment, but the
‘righteous into Life eternal.’

54. THE SCHOLAR.—Loving Master, please tell me *why Christ says*, ‘what you have done to the least of these you have done
‘to me; and what you have not done to them, neither have you
‘done it to me.’ And how does a Man do this *so*, as one who does
it to *Christ himself*?

THE MASTER.—*Christ* dwells *really* and essentially in the *Faith* of those that *wholly yield up* themselves to Him, and gives them His *Flesh* for *Food*, and His *Blood* for *Drink*; and thus possesses the *Ground* of their *Faith*, according to the *Interior* or *inward Man*. And a *Christian* is called a *Branch of the Vine Christ*, and a *Christian*, because *Christ* dwells *spiri-*

...effectually in him; therefore, whatever *good* anyone shall do for such a *Christian* in his bodily *necessities*, it is done for *Christ Himself* who *dwells in him*. For such a *Christian* is not *his own*, but is wholly surrendered to *Christ* and has become *His special possession*; consequently, the *good deed* is done to *Christ Himself*. Therefore, also, whoever shall withhold their *help* from such a *needy Christian* and refrain from serving him in his *necessity*, they thrust *Christ* away from themselves and despise *Him* in *His members*. When a *poor person* who belongs to *Christ* in this way asks anything of you, and you deny him in his *necessity*, you deny it to *Christ Himself*. And whatever *harm* anyone shall do to such a *Christian*, they do it to *Christ Himself*. When anyone mocks, scorns, reviles, rejects, or thrusts away such a person, they do all that to *Christ*; but he who receives him, gives him *food* and *drink* or *clothing*, and assists him in his *necessities*, does it likewise to *Christ* and to a *fellow member of his own body*. Indeed, he does it to himself if he is a *Christian*; for we are all *one* in *Christ*, just as a tree and its branches are *one*.

55. THE SCHOLAR.—How then will those survive on the *day* of that *fierce judgment* who *afflict* and *trouble* the *poor and distressed* and deprive them of the very fruits of their *sweat*, forcing and constraining them by *power* to be subject to their *wills*, and trampling upon them as their *footstools*, only so that they themselves may live in *pomp* and *power* and spend the *fruits* of these poor people’s *sweat* and *labor* in *sensuality*, *pride*, and *vanity*?

THE MASTER.—Christ *suffers* in the *persecution* of *His members*. Therefore, all the *wrong* that such harsh *exploiters* do to the *poor wretches* under their *control* is done to *Christ Himself* and falls under His *severe sentence* and *judgment*. And besides that, they help the *devil* to increase *his kingdom*; for by such *oppression* of the *poor*, they draw them away from *Christ* and make them seek *unlawful ways* to fill their bellies. Indeed, they work for and with the *devil* himself, doing the very same *thing* that he does; for he, without ceasing, opposes the *kingdom of Christ*, which consists only of *love*. All these *oppressors*, if they do not turn with their whole *hearts* to *Christ* and minister to or serve Him, must go into *hellfire*, which is *fed* and *kept alive* by nothing other than such *pure self-interest* as they have exercised over the *poor here*.

56. THE SCHOLAR.—But *how* will it go for *those*, and how will *they* be able to stand that *severe trial*, who at this time so fiercely argue about the *kingdom of Christ* and slander, revile, and persecute one another for their *religion*, as they do?

THE MASTER.—All such have not yet *known Christ* and are

<summary>The Master teaches that kindness shown to a believer is kindness shown to Christ, while the oppression of the poor and religious infighting are works of the devil that lead to eternal judgment.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the Master's discourse on spiritual judgment, shifting from the treatment of the poor to the nature of pride, sectarian contention, and the necessity of strife in the mortal world for the manifestation of divine wisdom.</meta>

<page-num>114</page-num>

but as a *Type* or *Figure* of *Heaven* and *Hell*, striving with each other for the Victory.

All rising, swelling *Pride*, which *contends* about *Opinions*, is an *Image of Self*. And whoever does not have *Faith* and *Humility*, nor lives in the *Spirit of Christ*, which is *Love*, is only armed with the *Anger of* GOD, and helps forward the victory of the *Imaginary Self*—that is, the *Kingdom of Darkness* and the *Anger of* GOD. For at the *Day of Judgment*, all *Self* shall be given to the *Darkness*, as shall also all the unprofitable *Contentions* of men, in which they seek not after *Love*, but merely after their *Imaginary Self*, so that they may exalt themselves by exalting and establishing their *Opinions*; stirring up *Princes* to *Wars* for the sake of those opinions, and by that means causing the *Desolation* of whole countries of people. All such things belong to the *Judgment*, which will separate the *false* from the *true*; and then all *Images* or *Opinions* shall cease, and all the *Children of God* shall dwell forever in the *Love of Christ*, and *That* in them.

All who in *this Time of Strife*—namely, from the *Fall* to the *Resurrection*—are not zealous in the *Spirit of Christ* and desirous to promote Peace and *Love*, but *seek and strive for themselves* only, are of the *Devil* and belong to the *Pit of Darkness*, and must consequently be separated from *Christ*. For in *Heaven*, all serve GOD their Creator in humble *Love*.

57. THE SCHOLAR.— *Why then does* GOD *allow such Strife and Contention to exist in this time?*

THE MASTER.— *Life* itself stands in *Strife*, so that it may be made *manifest*, *perceptible*, and *tangible*, and that *Wisdom* may be made *discernible* and *known*.

The *Strife* also constitutes the *eternal Joy* of the Victory. For there will arise *great Praise* and *Thanksgiving* in the *Saints* from the experiential sense and knowledge that *Christ in them* has overcome *Darkness* and all the *Self of Nature*, and that they are at length *totally* delivered from the *Strife*; in which they shall *rejoice eternally* when they shall know how the *Wicked* are recompensed. And therefore GOD *allows* all souls to stand in a *Free Will*, so that the *eternal Dominion* of both *Love* and *Anger*, of *Light* and of *Darkness*, may be made *manifest* and *known*; and that every *Life* might *cause* and *find* its own sentence within itself. For that which is now a *Strife* and *Pain* to the *Saints* in their wretched warfare *here* shall in the end be turned into *great joy for them*; and that which has been a *joy* and *pleasure* to *ungodly persons* in this world shall afterwards be turned into *eternal torment* and *shame for them*. Therefore the *Joy* of the *Saints* must arise for them out of *Death*, as the

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>The Master explains that prideful religious contentions serve the kingdom of darkness, but God allows strife to exist so that divine wisdom and the joy of victory can be made manifest through free will. Ultimately, the suffering of the saints will turn to eternal joy, while the selfish pleasures of the ungodly will turn to shame.</summary>
<keywords>Pride, Opinions, Image of Self, Spirit of Christ, Day of Judgment, Strife, Wisdom, Free Will, Eternal Dominion, Love and Anger</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the discourse on the super-sensual life, illustrating how spiritual light arises from the sacrificial fire of the self, continuing the Master's explanation of why strife is necessary for the manifestation of divine life.</meta>

<page-num>145</page-num>
# Of the Super-Sensual Life.

*Light* arises out of a *Candle* by the destruction and consump-
tion of it in its *fire*; so that the *life* may be freed from the
*painfulness* of *nature*, and possess another *world*.

And as the *light* has quite another property than the *fire*
has, for it *gives* and *yields* itself *forth*; whereas the *fire*
*draws in* and *consumes* itself; so the holy *life* of *meekness*
springs forth through the *death* of *self-will*, and then GOD’S
*will of love* only *rules*, and does all in all. For thus the
eternal *One* has attained *feeling* and *separability*, and brought
itself forth again with the *feeling*, through *death*, in great *joy-*
*fulness*; so that there might be an eternal *delight* in the infinite
*unity*, and an eternal *cause* of *joy*; and therefore *painfulness*
must now be the *ground* and *cause* of this *motion* or *stirring* to
the *manifestation* of all things. And herein lies the *mystery*
of the <unclear>hidden</unclear> *wisdom* of GOD.

---

->FINIS.<-

<summary>The author uses the analogy of a candle to explain how spiritual life and light emerge from the destruction of the self-will, concluding that worldly pain is the necessary foundation for the manifestation of eternal joy. This page marks the conclusion of the text on the Super-Sensual Life.</summary>
<keywords>Super-Sensual Life, divine light, self-will, meekness, Will of Love, divine unity, manifestation, wisdom of God</keywords>
